Kris's Compendium of Trade Goods (KCTG), A5e edition 1.32.2
By kristkos (https://patreon.com/KCTG), ported to Foundry A5e by Syrious (https://github.com/Syrious/kctg-a5e).
Used in CarpFinder with permission from Kris (Syrious), who must be credited.
Only the names, prices, weights and descriptions are used. None of the module's art may be used.

Extracted by .agent/port-scripts/kctg_extract.mjs. Do not edit by hand.
Prices are as printed, in 5e coins: 1 pp = 10 gp, 1 gp = 2 ep = 10 sp = 100 cp. Weights are in pounds.
Which of these prices CarpFinder items use is in .agent/port-scripts/trade_prices.py.

Each item is:  Name | price | weight | kind
               description

# PACK: A5e - KCTG - Trade Goods (1044 items)
####################################################################################################

== Adornments & Luxury / Coinage & Currency (20) ==

Ancient Gold | 2 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    These gold coins can be found in the deepest tombs or burial chambers. Kings or emperors were buried with jars filled with these types of coins. However, untrained adventurers quickly believe that they have the same value as a regular gold coin. Yet, these coins are 100% pure gold, and collectors will pay a high price for any amount of the ancient gold coins located.
Assaying Tool | 50 gp | 1 lb | loot
    It is commonly known as a Touchstone, a tool made from slate with a finely-grained surface on which metals leave a small trace. The touchstone is used by merchants and bank tellers along with equivalent metal leaves to identify the purity of coins, bars, or jewelry. The metal leaves, made of gold, silver, platinum, or electrum with various purity levels, are scratched on the touchstone along with the soon-to-be-acquired metal to determine its purity or whether it is a counterfeit.
Aurichalcum Coin | 1 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Aurichalcum Coins once saw widespread circulation in ancient times. However, in the more recent era, they are found only in remote regions with scarce access to primary metals such as gold or silver, where they are valued similarly to gold pieces.
Bear's Trace | 2 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    The Gleaming Unicorn and Golden Deer coins are considered valuable by some, but woodland folk refuse to touch them due to their metal properties. To create an alternative, the folk imbue a piece of soil with magic, and each minter uses their own animal form paw, claw, or hoof to create a unique mark in the mold as a signature. These coins have a value close to a copper due to their magical imbue, and multiple forgeries have been attempted. However, those familiar with the arcane arts can realize the coin is a counterfeit, or simply by adding water, as the coin is made of soil and it simply gets muddy. The magical imbuement also gives the coins water-repelling properties, making them harder to counterfeit.
Crimson Noble Slab | 50 gp | 2.2 lb | loot
    These specific slabs are made of 100% pure gold, weighing around 2.2 lbs, and for the longest time, they were simply known as Noble Gold Slabs. As people started using the slabs more frequently for nefarious deeds, they changed the name from Noble Gold Slabs to Crimson Noble Slabs. Nobility tends to use these for payment, as they are not marked with their family seals, to help sort out any forms of rivalry, from coups, and subversions to assassinations. These slabs are widely accepted by the majority merchants, who understand that they represent a completed transaction. Refusing them could potentially put them and their loved ones in danger.
Dark River Gold | 1 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    These coins, of unknown origin, are always discovered by fishermen, but when they appear, they are seen as a bad omen. After they are found in the rivers or seas, trade and communication with nearby cities slows down or stops, and rumors of monster invasions, tsunamis, and fires tend to emerge from the region. The source of these rumors is not yet verified. However, owners of the coins tend to quickly trade them for almost no profit, despite their rarity, possibly due to the perceived negative association.
Draconic Staters | 35 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    These coins, historically known as the first coins ever used, have undergone little modification throughout time. They are typically crafted by the minions of dragons, and always feature a carving of a dragon's wing or head. The dragons' hoards often contain varying qualities of precious metals, resulting in the coins being minted with a lower gold price compared to others on the market. Depending on the region, they may also be referred to as "wings" based on their symbol.
Elemental Debt | 0 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    This magical coin has an undefined value, which depends on the material used to create it. It is primarily used as a material that is about to be consumed for spells that have specific prerequisites. Magical alloys with pearls, diamonds, bones, and silver are commonly used to make these coins. Each coin bears the magical sygil representing the type of spell it can be used for, and this symbol also determines its value. These coins are typically found in major cities with a magical college that has the ability to craft them.
Favor Coin | 0 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    "Each favor has a price” stands etched on the side of the golden coin in a forgotten language. Usually traded and manufactured by nefarious groups for an underground economy. If a service is required, a coin must be paid. The services offered can range from a piece of vestment, advanced weaponry, to castle plans, to the swift disposal of bodies. All favors, all have a price.
Gleaming Unicorn | 25 sp | 0.01 lb | loot
    When forest folk attempt to create new forest circles, they use a form of primal magic to extract all the metals from the ground, and with the precious metals they dig out, they create coins. These coins are an alloy between platinum and silver making them a quite valuable commodity and used for trade when they go into civilization. Their value is roughly 25% of platinum.
Golden Deer | 25 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    When the forest folk finishes their druidic circles, a lot of precious metals they create coins from the alloys, from platinum and silver, as well gold and copper. The gold and copper alloy is named tumbaga, and in the bigger cities, they are commonly referred to as Tumbaga coins. They are worth roughly 25% of the gold's value. They are referred to as Deers or Elk, as it is the most common imprint on these coins.
Hunter's Mark | 10 gp | 0.1 lb | loot
    This uniquely shaped coin is given by nobles to hunters as identification for permission to hunt on their land, after some form of payment has been made. Once the hunt is complete, the hunter returns the coin to the noble and may choose to sell the hunt directly to them. Typically, the hunter and noble will agree to a percentage of the hunt's profits beforehand. The coin can also be sold to merchant banks at a higher price, freeing the noble to attend to other matters.
Metal Leaves | 10 gp | 1 lb | loot
    These are known as touchstone leaves, and are used by merchants or bank tellers to easily identify types of precious metals. The leaves themselves can be made out of gold, silver, platinum, or electrum, with various levels of purity. When testing a metal object, the leaves are scratched on a touchstone along with the object in question. Based on the residue they leave behind, it is possible to quickly identify the metal's purity or determine if it is a counterfeit.
Regal Mark | 1000 pp | 0 lb | loot
    When a king ascends to the throne, whether by succession, conquest, or assassination, a unique medallion coin is forged for them. This gold medallion features a dark-red diamond at its center, serving as a commemoration of the event and a means of quick identification if the king ever needs to blend in with their subjects. As each medallion is unique and only a limited number exist in circulation, some have the buying power to purchase anything on an entire continent.
Sea Daric | 3 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    A gleaming gold disc, emblazoned with a highly detailed carved skull. These coins are often crafted from pillaged gold obtained from various sources such as gold coins, jewelry, and gold bars. The weight of the coin itself is heavy, serving as a tangible reminder of the riches and dangers of the high seas. Throughout the minting process, the crafter ensures that the gold is as close to 100% pure as possible, making it one of the most valuable coins in circulation.
Seafinder Coin | 0 gp | 0.1 lb | loot
    A coin made for sea captains, for long trips, mainly used for superstition. Prior to setting sail on a lenghty journey, the captain will throw one of these coins into the waters to appease the gods or the sea itself depending on his beliefs. The coin is usually made out of silver with an gem that can range from Blue Topaz to the inexpensive Chrysocolla.
Siglos | 25 gp | 0.1 lb | loot
    These coins are crafted exclusively by monasteries for their parishioners who are merchants. They are typically made from stone and decorated with religious symbols and colors. The coins are distributed to merchants who travel to wealthier regions so that they can access services without having to pay extra. These services may include lodging, education, or meetings with the local high priests.
Silver Winter | 1 sp | 0.01 lb | loot
    This coin is a symbol of achievement and status within the bardic colleges. After successfully completing their last year of college, attendees are given a copper coin crafted by art students, featuring intricate seasonal markings. The coin is also available in other patterns, such as Silver Solar, Snow, or Summer, each corresponding to a different season. Despite its name, the coin is made of copper and holds a value similar to that of a silver piece.
Smith's Coin | 0 gp | 0.1 lb | loot
    When a customer places a custom order or commission at a specialty shop, such as a jewelry store or blacksmith, a coin bearing the shop's symbol is provided. The customer's name is etched on the back of the coin, and they are asked to sign a ledger. Once the item has been crafted or procured, the owner can retrieve it by trading in the coin. The value of the coin is equivalent to the cost of the commissioned item. If the coin is lost or stolen, it becomes worthless, and only the rightful owner can retrieve the item using the original coin.
Spider's Silk | 1 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Spider silk is highly valued and expensive, despite being a relatively abundant resource. Few people attempt to gather it due to the challenges involved. Although it sells for a high price, silk from larger spiders is usually a bit less costly. The harvesting process involves stunning the spiders and using a specialized extractor to collect the silk.

== Adornments & Luxury / Jewelry (28) ==

Agate Ring | 2 gp | 0 lb | loot
    The agate ring is rumored to attract wealth and prosperity. This belief may stem from Druidic traditions, which hold that the agate stone shares a strong connection and bond with the surrounding earth. Among the highest class of commoners, however, there exists a strict code of conduct regarding which finger and hand the agate gem should be worn on, a practice that changes depending on the day.
Blacksmith's Ring | 5 sp | 0.01 lb | loot
    After finalizing their training, newly appointed blacksmiths face one final challenge, the creation of the Blacksmith’s Ring. This task is more than ceremonial, as it marks the transition from a new apprentice to a skilled artisan. The ring itself is a solid iron band, engraved with the blacksmith’s family seal. The material, iron, chosen deliberately for its difficulty in delicate shaping and detailed engraving. It serves as one final lesson, as even the most unyielding material can be shaped into something precise, personal, and enduring.n the day.
Brooch | 15 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Brooch is a decorative jewelry item designed to be attached to garments, to fasten them together. It is usually made of metal, silver or gold or some other material, like clay. Brooches are frequently decorated with silver strands or with gemstones and may be solely for ornament and it can also serve a function as a clothes fastener.
Copper Band | 25 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    This simple metal ring is made out of copper. It's a great metal for making jewelry due to its ease of molding, durability, and unique reddish-brown color. Commoners attuned to spiritual practices often wear it with a strong belief, feeling it actively channels positive energy and attracts good fortune into their lives.
Diamond Arcana | 7500 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Created by the most skilled arcane masters and gem cutters. Formed from a single flawless diamond, the entire band is carved directly from the stone itself, shaped through forgotten arcane techniques rather than conventional tools. Each ring becomes part of a greater arcane economy, moving between powerful hands for very expensive spells.
Dragon Eyes | 0 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Dragon's Eyes are simple, yet gorgeous pieces of jewelry. These earrings are typically crafted as a pair that accompany a necklace and are bestowed exclusively upon royalty, traditionally during coronation ceremonies. The intricate craftsmanship required for these earrings is entrusted only to the most renowned gem cutters and jewelers. In the glint of the jewels, when light strikes them just so, some speculate that the watchful eye of an actual dragon might be glimpsed.
Dragon Heart | 0 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Dragon Heart is a pendant fit for an emperor, a masterpiece typically accompanied by its counterpart, the Dragon's Eyes earrings. The jewelry pieces are crafted from the same rare gemstone sharing the Dragon Heart name. Due to the gem's extreme rarity, only the most famous gem cutters and jewelers are permitted to cut and set these stones. The work made transforms raw rarity into symbols of godlike power.
Dragon Scale | 0 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    An actual dragon scale, attached to a piece of string, serves more as a symbolic trophy indicating that the owner is a dragon slayer. However, due to the rarity of dragon scales in the realms, not attached to the said dragons, collectors, and in some cases, more nefarious parties, may offer a significant premium to acquire it, depending on the color of the scale.
Dragon Touch | 35 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Dragon Touch ring is a scam ring piece, deliberately marketed as the missing third component to complete the prestigious set featuring 'Dragon's Eyes' earrings and the 'Dragon Heart' pendant. Primarily manufactured from Iron Pyrite and a cheap Agate, it does posses some value. However, the moment the piece of jewelry proves not to be what it was sold as, the silver-tongued scammer is nowhere to be seen.
Faction Earrings | 15 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    As a symbolic gesture, when joining very specific and well-renowned factions, a ring or a pair of earrings is provided as a welcome gift, along with other paraphernalia such as pens or notebooks bearing the same symbol. This helps quickly identify members and also gives the initial illusion of belonging.
Gem Encrusted Crown | 1250 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    A crown fit for a king. Usually crafted by a jeweler with a diverse set of skills, such as silversmithing, goldsmithing, and gemcutting, and often honored with the distinguished title of Crown Jeweler by the populace. He is also responsible for maintaining each piece of royal jewelry, ensuring it remains in pristine condition every time it is worn.
Gem Encrusted Polyhedral Dice | 125 sp | 0.01 lb | loot
    An encrusted dice set, typically containing multiple die types. Artisans carve elegant dice sets from luminous albite, inlaying each die with copper bindings to fortify its edges. These sets are usually accompanied by story-driven games played by commoners. Some of the more famous titles include the "Explorer" series as well as multiple editions of "Duties and Divinities" currently sitting at it's seventh edition.
Gold Bangle | 25 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Gold Bangle is a bracelet crafted from pure gold, designed to symbolize the highest class of nobility and the pinnacle of societal status. Forged by skilled blacksmiths using precise molds, each piece is then handed over to renowned goldsmiths who meticulously finish it with ornate detailing. These bangles are typically priced highly, which makes them frequent targets for thieves and swindlers seeking to profit from their value.
Gold Key | 10 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    To instill additional trust in the owners of safety deposit boxes across the realms, some banks commission keys for their boxes to be crafted from gold, projecting an image of prestige and wealth. However, these so-called gold keys are often fashioned from pyrite, a far less durable material. Over time and with regular use, these pyrite keys tend to snap or crumble, while the banks cutting costs.
Green Breeze Ear Piece | 1500 gp | 1 lb | loot
    This emerald-gemmed earpiece is designed for those who wish to convey their place among the upper class. It is an exceptionally expensive fashion accessory, though many find it uncomfortable to wear. The piece must be positioned on specific parts of the ear, which can be outright painful for some individuals whose ear shape does not accommodate its specific design fit.
Hair Pin | 25 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    A designer hairpin crafted with small gemstones to represent high status. It is made in such a way that, when it holds the hair, it exudes elegance and style, or to simply signal membership in the high noble class. These are primarily made by experienced jewelers and only on very high-priced commissions.
Jasper Ring | 75 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    The Jasper Ring is crafted from a single solid piece of jasper, shaped by experienced gemcutters with exceptional skill and precision. Thanks to its beautiful coloring and vibrant polish, it attracts the attention of many commoners seeking a ring that match their fingers. It is offered at a modest price, making it highly accessible to people from all walks of life.
Lifeline | 25 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Lifeline is a ring crafted by the most secluded underground folk, usually made in pairs, though larger sets are not unheard of. Its purpose is to keep in contact with friends or family. Primarily forged from iron or steel, the inscription on the inside contains the names of loved ones. With the aid of arcane knowledge, the ring forms a link to the others. When the text glows, it signals that the close family member or friend is still alive.
Marriage Band | 5 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Usually made out of gold, it is usually a religious symbol of bonding two in the eyes of a deity. The ring itself can be manufactured in multiple ways, with the addition of gems, inscriptions, or even alternative materials in the rare case that someone is allergic to gold or silver.
Moldavite Ring | 25 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Moldavite, a stone believed to have originated from celestial impacts, possesses a naturally glassy structure that allows it to be easily shaped into various implements such as rings or pendants. Some superstitious commoners claim that wearing moldavite jewelry significantly aids in healing otherwise incurable diseases, though these assertions remain unsupported by herbalists or alchemists.
Opal Earrings | 1250 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Cut by experienced gemcutters and connected with silver implements, opal, being one of the most colorful gems, when cut so precisely and in such a way that it provides a beautiful array of colors, becomes a favorite among the middle to higher classes of citizens. Some arcane casters usually wear the earrings, as they can be used as a conduit for some magical spells in their repertoire.
Pearl Earrings | 55 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    These earrings are typically made to order, using both common and exotic pearls such as freshwater, black, white, or silver. The cost of a commission vary wildly depending on the materials chosen. In some regions, they serve a ceremonial purpose, worn by senior clergy as part of their sacred vestments.
Royal Signet | 125 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Royal Signet is a piece of jewelry, typically custom-made for the nobility. The most renowned jewelers are usually commissioned for this precise work, as the impression it leaves in wax must be as detailed and clear as possible. The band is generally crafted from gold, while the signet itself is forged from a combination of alloys that can be replaced or reworked into the ring with ease if needed.
Silver Blue Spinel Ring | 750 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Dark blue spinel, a beautiful gem, is cut by skilled gemcutters and embedded into a silver or iron ring. Even though it is set in more affordable metals, the spinel itself elevates the ring's value to a level comparable with those made of more precious materials. Its rich hue of the gem, makes the ring highly desirable by nobility.
Silver Earings | 125 sp | 0.01 lb | loot
    To avoid the high prices of gem-inlaid implements, earrings made solely from silver are also crafted. Aside from being a precious metal, silver offers the added benefit of being easier to mold, allowing artisans to create intricate patterns and detailed designs, while keeping the cost accessible to the average commoner.
Silver Ring | 1 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Silver rings tend to serve primarily as fashion statements, often chosen to complement specific outfits through visual coordination. They are also worn by individuals who are allergic to certain metals or by those seeking a more affordable option when purchasing new jewelry.
Stones Of Mother Earth | 125 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    These earrings are made with diopside, and their name is taken from the reverence druids hold for this particular stone in high regard. The beautiful light green gem, set in gold, creates an allure of elegance and an air of nobility. The price reflects this appearance, aligning with the refined impression they leave.
The Forest | 150 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    The Forest is a necklace crafted from copper and set with a zoisite implement. While copper is a relatively inexpensive material, the presence of the zoisite balances the value, resulting in a piece that has a surprisingly high price. Yet, because copper is somewhat fragile, these necklaces are often found in crowded places, having slipped from their wearers' necks after the copper has corroded.

== Adornments & Luxury / Vessels & Tableware (10) ==

Copper Kettle | 1 gp | 3 lb | loot
    Hammered copper with a tight lid, a spout, and an arched handle, made for boiling water over a hearth or a campfire faster than any iron pot. Inns keep one hanging over the fire from dawn until late at night for tea, shaving water, and washing up. Hard water leaves a white crust on the inside that cooks scrub out with vinegar and sand.
Copper Saucepan | 5 sp | 2 lb | loot
    Beaten from a single copper plate, tinned on the inside, with an handle long enough to keep your hand clear of the fire. Copper heats far faster than iron, making it a bit more viable, and cheaper, than its iron counterpart. The tin lining wears away under constage usage and has to be renewed, work that traveling tinkers can do for a few coppers.
Crock | 5 cp | 3 lb | loot
    Glazed clay jar with a cloth cover tied down over the mouth, used for salting meat, souring milk, and keeping anything that has to breathe but must not be opened for a longer time. The fabric is replaced often and a household keeps a stack of cut cloth squares for the purpose.
Demijohn | 5 gp | 10 lb | loot
    Large glass bottle with a narrow neck, encased in woven wicker to protect it from knocks, holding several gallons of wine, oil, or vinegar. Merchants ship them by the wagonload packed in straw, and taverns fill their jugs and flasks from them. An empty one is never thrown away, as the glass often costs more than what it held.
Golden Encrusted Goblet | 55 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Throughout history and in certain fringe regions of the realms, goblets served as a symbol of status and refinement. Alchemists and herbalists have long warned against drinking alcohol from gold goblets, as the metal can react with the contents and produce toxic effects. Today, such goblets are most often discovered in forgotten tombs or among the hoards of exotic collectors, winged or otherwise.
Iron Stewpot | 2 gp | 10 lb | loot
    Iron cast with a rolled rim and two handles, thick enough that the bottom will not burn through over a fire going all day. It is the most common cooking vessel in the realms and the one most often named outright in a will, since a good pot outlasts the person who bought it. However, the more larger ones, are used to bathe infants and children, as their size is perfect for this specific task.
Lidded Tureen | 10 gp | 6 lb | loot
    Glazed white porcelain with a gold rim and a fitting lid, made to carry soup, stews, and many hot dishes, without it going cold on the way. Most households, usually, have a matching set of porcelain tableware for display in cupboards. The lid is the piece that often breaks, and a replacement almost never matches the body, however, some specilized potters can fit the broken pieces with gold.
Silver Goblet | 125 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Silver goblets, along with other pieces such as plates, platters, cutlery, and eating utensils, are ever-present in a noble household. These items are often commissioned in bulk, typically from a dedicated smithy or the local blacksmith. In most cases, the noble provides the silver ingots required for the commission, having full control over the final product.
Waterskin | 2 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Entire leather hide sewn into a bag, pitched on the inside and fitted with a horn or wooden tip, and usually carried on a strap over the shoulder. New ones taint the water for the first week or two till the leather cleans fully. The seams fail first and the skin is worth repairing several times prior to it is cut up for patches.
Wooden Bowl | 2 cp | 0 lb | loot
    Carved from hardwoods, wooden bowls are both aesthetically pleasing and practical, known for their heat retention and easy handling. Nevertheless, their porous nature can complicate cleaning and maintenance, necessitating special care.

== Antiquities & Relics / Ceremonial & Sacred (14) ==

Burnt Offerings | 5 cp | 0.5 lb | loot
    Bundles of herbs, and incense bound together for burning at temple altars. The clergy purchase them in decent quantities, for larger shrines, or churches, by commissioning in bulk from herbalists. Explorers venturing into old tombs often encounter charred remnants at altar sites.
Ceremonial Dagger | 50 gp | 0.5 lb | loot
    Never intended for combat, these either bronze or electrum blades with ornate handles serve as ritual tools during sacrifices or oaths. Temples commission them from specialized smiths whose work must pass high-class religious scrutiny before being purchased, let alone, considered consecrated. Well preserved examples fetch prices far beyond their weight in metal with antiquarians.
Ceremonial Plate | 15 gp | 2 lb | loot
    Hammered from bronze or gilded copper, these plates carry offerings to temple altars or used as burning vessels for them. Their surfaces are typically embossed with divine iconography or the names of deities. Old examples unearthed from tomb antechambers fetch a pretty coin from private collectors.
Ceremonial Tablet | 25 gp | 5 lb | loot
    Flat slabs of fired clay or carved stone bearing inscriptions of prayers, funerary rites, or dedications to divine patrons. Scribes trained in sacred texts prepare them on behalf of nobles and priests for burials. Scholars and historians want intact examples recovered from tombs as they are invaluable pieces of religious history.
Divine Cat Statuette | 50 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Figures made of faience, bronze, or gold representing a feline deity venerated across desert regions. Devotees place them in household shrines as protective wards against vermin and ill fortune. Intact examples from sealed tombs make collectors of religious antiquities really happy.
Gold Goblet | 75 gp | 0.5 lb | loot
    Used in temple rites to hold sacred wine, oil, or ceremonial waters, gold goblets are symbols of piety or wealth, usually both, rather than everyday vessels. Their primary purpose is display during major religious festivals or nobility funerals.
Golden Coffer | 100 gp | 3 lb | container
    Small golden boxes used to transport sacred objects, priestly gear, and offerings, adorned with divine symbols or family heirlooms. In burial practice they are placed alongside the deceased to carry the items in their afterlives.
Golden Priest Statuette | 200 gp | 2 lb | loot
    Gilded figurines depicting priests in formal vestments, placed in temples as perpetual stand-ins for devotion meant to intercede with the divine on the donor's behalf. Intact pieces fetch a really high price, as fine detail work rarely survives centuries.
Grain Offerings | 2 cp | 0.5 lb | loot
    Simple bundle of harvested grain stalks, sometimes consecrated by a temple priest, representing the first fruits of the harvest returned to the gods. One of the most common offerings at rural shrines or religious festivals, purchased or harvested by local farmers.
Snake Gold Piece | 25 gp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Small gold statuette of a coiled serpent, cast by cults or regional power. Smelters accept them by weight, the purity of the gold speaking louder than whatever faith once backed them. Collectors prize intact examples with fine detail work, sometimes paying several times their melt value.
Sun Disc | 100 gp | 3 lb | loot
    Hammered gold disc with a chiseled stylized face, hung above altars or carried at the head of a procession to catch the light. Temples commission them at a fixed weight by the rite rather than by what the metal costs, so two discs out of the same sanctuary will match each other exactly. Melted down they are worth their value in the metal and not more, which has never stopped anyone from trying to steal them, well, that is, if they can carry it.
Usekh | 75 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Broad decorative collar of ancient origin composed of multiple rows of faience beads, gold shapes, or colored stone covering the chest and upper shoulders. The finest antique examples incorporate lapis lazuli, carnelian, and turquoise in gold mounts. Imitation versions are produced for theatrical performances and festive occasions throughout the realms.
Wesekh | 100 gp | 0.5 lb | loot
    Similar to the usekh but favoring flat, closely fitted plates of hammered electrum or gold engraved with divine inscriptions rather than gems and plates. Priests of the old rites get these for festival days and dedicatory holidays.
Winged Pharaoh Necklace Piece | 150 gp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Pendant cast in gold and inlaid with semiprecious stones, depicting a symbol-like falcon or vulture with wings spread wide. Single pieces surface regularly in trade, separated from their original settings by tomb robbers. Jewelers skilled in the old style of gold molding, pay well to acquire matched sets for reconstruction.

== Antiquities & Relics / Funerary & Memorial (8) ==

Humanoid Remains | 5 sp | 10 lb | loot
    Scattered bones find niche but persistent trade with medical practitioners studying anatomy and arcane traditions requiring skeletal components for binding. Those who dwell in the unliving arcane arts are the primary buyers at any price above token. In some cultures the bones of notable individuals are preserved as relics.
Memorial Signet | 15 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Signet plaque engraved with the name or symbol of the deceased individual, or his guild, commissioned by families to mark the passing of a notable member. Unlike functional seals, these are placed in tombs or displayed in family shrines as markers of lineage or guild affiliation.
Memorial Stone | 50 gp | 50 lb | loot
    Carved monument bearing the name, titles, deeds, and the likeness of the deceased, shaped to last centuries in granite or basalt. Stonemasons specializing in funerary work receive steady commissions from noble households and wealthy merchant families.
Ossuary Box | 8 sp | 12 lb | loot
    Wooden chest with an detacheable iron ring on the lid, sized to hold the bones of one person once the flesh is gone and the grave is needed again. Carpenters make them to a fixed measure so they will stack on a shelf, and the name is cut into the sides. Where burial ground is scarce this becomes an necessity.
Stone Memorial | 25 gp | 30 lb | loot
    Cut from granite, limestone, or sandstone, stone memorials range from simple name plaques to large decorated stelae bearing biographical inscriptions. Historians and archaeologists frequently cite them as primary source material for major events.
The King's Ring | 500 gp | 0 lb | loot
    Gold band bearing the cartouche or personal seal of a ruler, serving in life as an unimpeachable mark of royal authority and in death as proof of identity before the divine. A ring of this type recovered from a sealed burial carries significant value, though collectors are wise to remember that the claim it represents does not die with its first owner.
Tomb Stone | 5 sp | 15 lb | loot
    Flat or upright slab of stone bearing little more than a name and occasionally a date of death, common in cemeteries and roadside burial grounds across all realms. Local stonemasons produce them in large quantities at low cost, more as a courtesy to grieving commoners than seeing it as a profitable trade.
Wooden Cross | 2 cp | 2 lb | loot
    Funerary marker used across many cultures to denote a place of burial, cut from hardwood by any competent carpenter at cheap cost. Over time they rot and lean, requiring periodic maintenance and replacement. Certain religious orders commission specialist carpenters destined for notable graves.

== General Trade / Animals & Livestock (35) ==

Acorns | 1 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Gathered by the sack, under oak trees every autumn, mostly by the young folk, and sold as fodder to fatten pigs before the winter slaughter. Some landowners charge a fee for every pig turned loose in their woods to eat them, to contain the trees, and count the pigs by the gate. In some areas the commoners soak the bitterness out of them in running water and grind them into a coarse flour for bread.
Aphid | 0 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Aphids are tiny insects that survive by sucking sap from plants. Their presence can lead to serious crop damage, and without intervention, they pose a threat to farms, trees, and gardens alike. Commoners dread any appearance of this little critters.
Carpet Beetle | 0 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Carpet Beetles are almost everywhere, and it is likely that every farmer hearth has a few. As scavengers, they consume dead insects, fur, hair, skin, and other decaying organic matter. They pose no threat to living beings and neither bite nor sting, in the more abstract sciences, herbalists tend to use them to estimate the time of death.
Centipede | 0 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Centipedes enter homes seeking food, water, and shelter, same as the common folk, particularly in damp and dark environments. They are attracted to areas with high humidity, such as basements, crawl spaces, and bathrooms, and they hunt other insects for food. While centipedes can bite and inject venom, they are generally not considered dangerous to common folk.
Chick | 2 cp | 1 lb | loot
    The chick does not have many uses at this point in its lifespan. However, those who manage the coops tend to separate them for future use, either for meat or for eggs. To earn a little income, the farmer will take some of the chicks to the market for sale.
Chicken | 2 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Known as poultry, chickens are typically kept by commoners for their reliable supply of eggs to sustain the entire household. The meat is mainly reserved for special occasions like birthdays or holidays, as it does become the centerpiece for a very filling meal.
Cow | 10 gp | 1400 lb | loot
    Cows are considered one of the most farmed animals, they are precious to farmhands as none of their materials go to waste. From staple products like meat or dairy to hides for leather, glue extracted from the hide and bones, to the crafting of silverware, pins, fishhooks, flutes, or bone needles, every aspect of a cow's harvesting is utilized to its fullest.
Dobsonfly | 0 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Dobsonfly is a very large insect, insect that is born from a larva known as the hellgrammite. It feeds on aquatic insects, nectar, sugar water, worms, and even small fish. While the huge size of it, can be intimidating. Dobsonfly’s brief lifespan of about a week and its nocturnal habits mean that many commoners may go their whole lives without encountering one.
Duck | 8 sp | 8 lb | loot
    Primarily raised for their meat, ducks also yield eggs of higher value as they are quite nutritious. Commoners often keep them around the house for an extended period compared to regular hens, as ducks don't need elaborate housing, resistant to diseases, and efficiently remove small pests like snails or slugs.
Dung Beetle | 0 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Dung Beetle their appearance does show that a pasture is quite healthy, as they are perfect in breaking down and burying animal waste, improving the soil. While, a bit controvesial, in some regions, they are used in cooking. After capturing they are cleaned, roasted, and dehydrated, sometimes with added seasonings. Some recipes involve trimming the wings and legs of the beetles, then stir-frying them with oil, basil, chiles, and lemongrass until crispy.
Feed Trough | 1 gp | 40 lb | loot
    Large hollowed log or planked box set on the ground for watering and feeding stock, wide enough that several animals can eat or drink from it without crowding each other out. Water rots the wood from the inside, so a trough is turned over and dried out whenever the herd is moved.
Goat | 8 gp | 175 lb | loot
    Goats have been utilized for milk, meat, fur, and skins across most of the realms. Goat milk is often processed into goat cheese, and farmers sell it at a premium. The hide and fabric are perfect for making gloves, shoes, and other accessories, making it a favorite for leatherworkers.
Green Soldier Bug | 0 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Green Soldier Bug, commonly referred to as the stink bug, emits a foul odor when crushed and poses a serious threat to crops like aubergines and tomatoes. Fortunately, unlike the Green Summer beetle, this critter can be managed effectively by hens and ducks, which help control their numbers without harming any of the crop.
Green Summer Beetle | 0 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Green Summer beetles become a problem during the growing season, harming both gardens and crops. These beetles target ripe or fermenting fruits like figs, grapes, and berries. Every summer, many common folk try to include wormwood in their planting cycle as it mostly behaves as an insect repellent.
Hay Bale | 5 cp | 30 lb | loot
    Cut grass dried in the field, turned until it will not heat, and bound tight for storage through the winter months. Farmers sell what they can spare in autumn and the unprepared ones, buy it back at three times the price in spring. Most pastures have a very large supply.
Hellgrammite | 0 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Hellgrammites are the perfect fishing bait across the realms, known to attract a wide range of fish including panfish, perch, pickerel, channel catfish, and especially bass. When fishing in deeper water or near heavy structures like submerged logs, tree roots, large rocks, or boat docks, they are thought to be particularly successful at drawing in the big catches, however, quite intimidating to look at.
Hog | 10 gp | 200 lb | loot
    Referred to as the pig, more commonly known as hog or sow in husbandry, this animal holds a prominent position as one of the most farmed for its meat. It serves as a vital resource in the production of sausages, gammon, bacon, ham, and various other food items.
Hugger Of Faces | 0 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    According to some rare studies, the creature’s body wraps tightly to the face, while its tail coils around the neck, to an unknown purpose. However, due to limited documentation of them, their purpose remains uncertain. No living specimens have ever been discovered, only the ocassional exoskeleton shells. Analysis of these shells has led to the theory that the creature's blood is highly acidic as it is capable of dissolving most substances.
Ladybug | 0 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Ladybugs, associated with luck due to their spots, always follow a plentiful harvest. Common folk love them because they eat aphids and other plant pests, keeping the crops safe. The kids love to look at them and have a small song to accompany it, used to count the spots on the ladybug, believing they bring them long lasting luck.
Lobster | 0 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Lobsters live in all oceans, on rocky, sandy, or muddy bottoms from the shoreline to the deep sea. Lobsters, like some snails and spiders, have blue blood, which alchemists extract for use against some of the more common illnesses and to heal skin damaged by burns.
Macaw | 2 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    The Macaw, one of the rarer types of parrots, is highly favored by seafaring rogues. These birds keep a watchful eye on the crew, relay commands, and update the captain on the ship's activities while he rests.
Mule | 40 gp | 480 lb | loot
    Mules, widely used as working animals in the realms, are highly prized for their hardiness and docile nature. Their strength for their size and superior physical endurance compared to most horses, coupled with the ability to survive extreme weather, increased patience, and longer lifespan, make them a favorite choice for traveling very long distances.
Owl | 5 gp | 4 lb | loot
    Owls, symbols of wealth, prosperity, wisdom, good luck, and fortune, are sought after as pets by many nobles and individuals dabbling in the arcane arts. Those engaged in animal husbandry, successfully domesticating owls, soon realize their effectiveness in controlling rodent infestations.
Pill Woodlouse | 0 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    It goes by many names, including common pill-bug, slaters, common pill woodlouse, roly-poly, potato bug, doodle bug, and carpenter woodlouse. Some farmers keep them after a successful harvest to clean the soil, as they feed on dead plants and prevent the spread of weeds.
Purple Emperor | 0 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Purple Emperor is an magnificent butterfly which flies high in the treetops of very dense forests. Alchemists, usually keep a few in a butterfly house for breeding, as they are an rare reagent in some of the most rare potions.
Rabbit | 5 sp | 4 lb | loot
    Commonly referred to as the meat rabbit, these rabbits are primarily harvested for their meat. However, in regions where hunting was not readily available, both wild rabbits and domestic rabbits were also harvested for pelts.
Red Paper Wasp | 0 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Red Paper is a non-hostile type of wasp that becomes aggressive only when its nest is threatened. Their venom can be collected by herbalists to produce medicine, safely harvested due to the insect’s docile behavior.
Saddlebags | 4 gp | 8 lb | container
    Pair of leather bags joined by a wide strap and slung behind the saddle, one on each side of the horse, closed with buckled flaps. Couriers, traveling merchants, and anyone riding long distances use them, as they hold far more than a rider could carry on their back. They have to be packed evenly, since an unbalanced pair slowly drags the saddle to one side over the course of a ride.
Scorpion | 0 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Scorpions are nocturnal hunters that can be found mainly in dry regions, hiding under rocks and within burrows during the day. Their iconic tail carries a venomous stinger used to subdue prey and defend against threats. While their sting is painful to common folk, it is rarely fatal, and alchemists seek their venom for use in various potent medicine concoctions.
Snapping Death Worm | 0 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Snapping Death Worm, or simply called Bobbit, is an subterranean critter that burrows mostly on beaches, however, every recolection of him resembles a horror story. It senses vibrations on the surface to ambush prey, launching itself upward to drag creatures underground with its snapping jaws.
Spider Beetle | 0 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    While the Spider Beetle is harmless to people, spotting it near granaries are problematic. They feed on the dried food supplies, particularly grains and fruits, seeing it, usually, it shows that stored provisions have gone bad.
Ticks | 0 cp | 0 lb | loot
    Ticks are insects that latch onto passing animals and common folk to feed on their blood. They are found in tall grasses and wooded areas, waiting for a host to brush by. Everytime a herbalist has to handle an tick, as they are known carriers of diseases, it asks the farmers to check all livestock that they own as well.
Travel Horse | 75 gp | 480 lb | loot
    A riding horse, or a saddle horse, is a horse used by mounted riders for recreation or transportation. It offers freedom, and movement, and enables some amazing feats of athleticism. There is a total thrill in galloping across an open field, in tune with your mount.
White Grubs | 0 cp | 0 lb | loot
    White Grubs are the larvae form of multiple beetles, found just beneath the soil. They feed on the roots of grasses and plants, causing dead patches in pastures and gardens. Though a bane to most farms, anglers value them as exceptionally effective bait.
Worm | 0 cp | 0 lb | loot
    Worms are an common sight in healthy soil, mainly after heavy rains. They consume decaying roots making them a good sight, and a hope of good harvest, in any garden or farm. They are also widely used by commoners and fishermen as reliable bait for fishing.

== General Trade / Documents & Scrolls (38) ==

Abjurating Abjuratives In Abjuration | 0 gp | 0 lb | loot
    "Abjuration is the solemn repudiation, abandonment, or renunciation by or upon oath, often the renunciation of citizenship or some other right or privilege," appears to be written in bold letters on the first page. The following pages contain examples of materials used and the resulting variations of spells. This seems to be a book written by a minstrel, intended to provide a deeper understanding of abjuration and its specific applications in arcane knowledge.
Advanced Military Application Of The Arcane Arts | 0 gp | 0 lb | loot
    A small manual that delves into military strategies for the tactical use of arcane arts. It contains multiple chapters covering combat topics such as group formations, flanking techniques, tips for surviving when lost behind enemy lines, essential survival skills, and, in the final chapter, the importance of the chain of command. This manual is issued to newly recruited soldiers, who are expected to memorize all 200 pages of it.
Anatomy Worksheet | 0 gp | 0 lb | loot
    Anatomy Worksheet is a specialized sheet that details all the body parts of a creature. From tissues to cranial nerves, from articulations to skeletal muscles, each component is described and drawn in extensive detail to give the reader a full understanding of how the living body functions. The Anatomy Worksheet is used not only for academic study but also for practical applications such as medical treatment, arcane healing, or creature crafting.
Book Of Fables | 0 gp | 0 lb | loot
    A series of fantastical stories written by multiple authors. Young readers hold a few of these tales dear to their hearts, particularly the animal-centered ones, one which follows a rabbit and a turtle and another tells of a fox trying to reach some grapes.
Book Of Pawns | 0 gp | 0 lb | loot
    "Book of Pawns" is a humorously named tome containing a collection of puns designed to make even the most stoic individuals groan. Some prime examples include: "What happens if you ask a treant for life advice? He’ll tell you to branch out and leave." or "Why did the kobold’s stand-up act bomb? - Their jokes drag on."
Brotherhood Of The North | 0 gp | 0 lb | loot
    Identified as a historical book, most of its contents have yet to be fully translated. Historians, with each revision, attempt to update the book with their latest findings, however, it appears to be written in an extinct language from millennia ago. From the currently available translations, the text seems to consist mostly of poems describing battles for Nordic territories. Yet, even these interpretations are heavily debated, as the accuracy of the translations remains uncertain.
Calendar | 0 gp | 0 lb | loot
    Simple tracker for the days of multiple seasons, clearly indicated by symbols or drawings, allows regular commoners to easily track what specific date it is in the current season and how long is left until the end. This can be used to track specific events like harvest, spring, or any other festivals.
Carriage Safety Guide | 0 gp | 0 lb | loot
    Small safety scroll that contains information of the next carriage trip, with points such as: Smoking Area: While we acknowledge the confusion, this area is not intended for the consumption of exotic herbs. It is specifically designated for humanoids of elemental nature. Please refrain from any form of extinguishing in this area.Metal, Heavy, and Large Creatures: If you are a creature composed of metal, of significant size, or a magic user who carries extensive inventory in compact spaces (e.g., extra-dimensional storage), please inform us prior to boarding. Specialized equines may be required. Failure to notify us will result in extended baggage fees.Clawed Humanoids: Please refrain from scratching any seats or wooden parts of the carriage.Horned Humanoids: Horned individuals must keep their horns corked at all times unless their horns curve inward toward themselves.Transmutation Casters: Do not transmute any liquids into alcohol. Following a previous incident, all alcohol is strictly forbidden during the trip. Additionally, please ignore any screaming rocks encountered during the journey.Full Moon Advisory: Passengers with lycanthropy symptoms are advised not to book trips during a full moon. Should you choose to ignore this warning, be aware that you may be permanently hired to replace any equines lost during the journey.Aura Bearers: Passengers with an active aura must remain in the specifically sanctioned areas. We understand the importance of your religion, deity, or patron, but we must consider the comfort of all travelers, including those from other cultures, or those recently returned from the afterlife. Noncompliance will be reported to the nearest guard post.Petrifying Creatures: If your species can petrify others through sight or other means, you must notify staff before boarding. Failure to do so will result in the installation of mirrors around your seat for the duration of the trip.Food Consumption: No food may be consumed during the journey. Multiple rest stops will be provided where you may eat. Other passengers are not considered food, regardless of cultural practices.Safety Notice: Keep all limbs inside the carriage at all times. We are not responsible for any injuries sustained during the journey.
Certificate Holder Cylinder | 0 gp | 0 lb | loot
    Simple cylinder designed to hold a large amount of documentation. It is usually carried by tax collectors or couriers to transport a large quantity of papers or documents while traveling. In archives, cylinders are used to store records, helping to occupy less space and be better for organization. These cylinders are often made from durable materials, such as thick leather, metal, or reinforced parchment, to withstand wear and protect important documents during transit.
Codex Micologica - A Guide To Mushrooms | 0 gp | 0 lb | loot
    A detailed booklet that showcases the most common mushrooms, categorized by climate, region, and weather. The Codex provides extensive information with clear indications of which mushrooms are safe to eat, safe to brew, which to avoid, and how to store them for extended periods. Carried as a companion both in the wild and in the alchemists' laboratories.
Codex Of Hatred & Love | 0 gp | 0 lb | loot
    Small journal titled this way by Valentin Wolfve contains his personal musings on how both love and hatred serve distinct purposes across various fields such as art and research, while he entirely dismisses regret. Several excerpts suggest that through the eyes of a loved one, the most beautiful portraits in history were created, while through hatred, the most in-depth research was pursued solely to discredit opposing causes. As for regret, he simply notes: "One thing you cannot change is what is already done."
Contract | 0 gp | 0 lb | loot
    A written contract between two parties that outlines the specifics for a job, trade, or other arrangement. It is signed by both parties and stamped with a seal by a third party as a witness. The contract can include as many details as required for the request at hand. It is typically provided in three copies, one for each party involved.
Cypher - Text | 0 gp | 0 lb | loot
    This cylinder often serves as a secure container, designed to protect valuable or sensitive items, ensuring that only those with the correct knowledge can open it. They are usually made with written details on a cylinder, the instructions describe the steps required to open and access the item inside. Breaking the cipher open carries a high risk of triggering a fail-safe that could destroy the item within.
Dictionary | 0 gp | 0 lb | loot
    This book contains all the words in a language along with their specific definitions. It is especially helpful for those attempting to learn the language, uncover archaic terms or regionalisms, and to also resolve complex puzzles.
Dungeon Plans | 0 gp | 0 lb | loot
    A dungeon plan schematic contains the full layout of a specific dungeon, either already built or planned to be constructed beneath a castle, fort, or large dwelling, such as a manor. Dungeons are typically used to hold prisoners until a trial. While the dungeon itself is not intended for long-term confinement, it is not unheard of for prisoners to remain there for extended periods. The schematic often includes areas specifically designed for interrogation, storage of evidence, or other purposes tied to the ruling authority’s needs.
Equipment Order | 0 gp | 0 lb | loot
    An official edict calling for a large quantity of gear, including both armor and weapons, is typically issued as a preparation for war or to support an ongoing conflict. In return, blacksmiths submit a material order, estimating the necessary amounts of supplies to fulfill the request.
Expensive Fish & Where To Get Them | 0 gp | 0 lb | loot
    The book quickly delves into details about various fishing areas; however, much of the information appears incomplete or misleading. It is commonly sold in taverns by self-proclaimed famous fishermen, often targeting unskilled commoners as part of a get-rich-quick scheme. The basic premise encourages readers to simply head to the nearest body of water, catch whatever they can, and sell it for profit. Throughout the book, a recurring tagline is used as an incentive: "One catch can change your life. Here's how!"
Faded Map | 0 gp | 0 lb | loot
    Faded map which points to an unknown location, the text is too indecipherable to distinguish any cities, regions, or forest names. A highly skilled cartographer might provide some insight on the specifics of the map itself, such as when it was made based on the paper or ink used.

    "North Noria was once the epicenter of the region plagued by Kah'Yul’s influence. In response, Cleric Anilisia, chief of the Red Obsidian, sought to plant a Young Virathryn sapling, a sacred tree said to bridge the mortal world to Kah'Yul's realm. She planted it within the overground chambers, believing that its growth toward the sky would forge the connection she needed. However, her not-so-friendly sisters, who opposed her plan, had already made their move, poisoning the soil in secret. Days later, Cleric Tilena, chief among the dissenters, mocked Anilisia’s efforts. In a cruel display, Tilena reached into the corrupted earth, unearthed the blackened Virathryn seed, and crushed it in her hand. Fearing Anilisia’s wrath, Tilena cast powerful illusions to mask her escape and fled into the wilds." The chapter ends here. The remaining chapters of this chronicle seem lost to time, their secrets buried with them.

    A potion recipe that supposedly makes one quite lucky with just one sip. While the actual effectiveness of the potion is debatable, the recipe has already undergone eleven different revisions. It's rather popular among the nobility.
Invitation | 0 cp | 0 lb | loot
    Invitations are often used for weddings, official events, or to gain access to the count's court. They vary greatly in style, ranging from highly formal to personal, and from luxurious to official. It’s typically difficult to know exactly what kind of event awaits until the invitation is opened.

    Monstrous Monsters is a small manual offered by tavernkeepers to travelers, adventurers, caravaners, and merchants alike. Permits | 0 gp | 0 lb | loot
    Usually not officially named as permits, they go by various titles such as writs, charters, licenses, or letters patent. Examples include a License to Bear Arms, which allows individuals to carry weapons in the presence of nobility, or a Permit to Travel or Safe Conduct license, often granted to envoys tasked with delivering messages or goods. Another example is a Resource Extraction license, which grants rights to harvest timber from a designated area of a noble’s forest. Ore, meat, and other resources also fall under the same type of license, depending on the material being gathered.
