    Scheelite gemstones are found in shades ranging from pale yellow to orange and are suitable for faceting, slicing, and polishing, making them ideal for inlay work. Larger, irregular specimens are commonly mounted in silver as pendants or used for furniture ornamentation.
Serpentine | 50 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Serpentine, sometimes referred to as "Poor man's Jade", is a relatively common gemstone. It is typically cut into round or oval shapes known as cabochons, which are then used to adorn weapons, armor, or jewelry.
Silver Pearl | 220 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Even if it's referred to as silver, it usually bears a green tint based on the biome it was harvested from. Mainly used by alchemists to create potions that improve the user's attention and survival skills.
Sinhalite | 100 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Sinhalite, occurring naturally, manifests as yellow gemstones resembling rounded pebbles, shaped by the tumbling action of water in active streams or dried-up riverbeds.
Smithsonite | 50 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Smithsonite, also referred to as zinc spar, is frequently discovered in zinc ore deposits. In earlier eras, it was believed to possess the remarkable ability to restore sight to the blind, though the ancient methods for achieving this feat have faded into obscurity. Modern alchemists persist in their quest to uncover and revive these lost arts.
Spessartine | 125 gp | 0.02 lb | loot
    Spessartine is commonly found in deposits alongside Morion, often occurring in substantial quantities. Ancient lore recounts its use in forging full suits of armor, a feat attributed to skilled gem cutters with arcane knowledge
Sphene | 500 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Sphene is known for its soft and brittle nature, found in shades ranging from yellow to deep green, with the prized emerald green hue being the most sought-after. Its crystals are often cut into dazzling faceted gems of small to medium size.
Star Rose Quartz | 50 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Or simply known as Rose Quartz, is a pink gemstone mainly used in jewelry. The large slabs can be cut into multiple shapes from hearts, for holidays and anniversaries to huge round shapes, for wizards' crystal balls. It can also be used as a magical material, to deflect spells.
Stibiotantalite | 100 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Stibiotantalite is primarily valued as a gemstone, known for its vivid, shiny, golden-brown appearance. It was commonly worn by merchants and mid-level courtiers as an affordable alternative to more expensive gemstones.
Tabasheer | 50 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Tabasheer is an opal-like type of stone found in the nodes of specific bamboo. This stone is not used for ornamentation but is valued by herbalists for medicinal purposes, aiding in the treatment of symptoms such as fevers, wounds, or uncontrollable vomiting, whether caused by arcane sources or otherwise.
Tanzanite | 175 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    The Tanzanite gemstone, a type of zoisite, is known for its deep blue color and unique optical properties. It is one of the few gemstones that can display three different colors depending on the viewing angle. Tanzanite appears as a distinct blue when viewed from one direction and shifts to hues ranging from violet to red when viewed from other angles.
Tektite | 50 gp | 0.1 lb | loot
    When rocks fall from the heavens and smash mountains, the small-sized rocks that are charred are called tektites. However, most alchemists are curious about the overall process at the moment of impact, as it turns the small rocks into glass. Tektite is mainly used as furniture ornamentation as its shade of blue is quite pleasant to the eye.
Tiger's Eye | 10 gp | 0 lb | loot
    One of the more common gemstones out there, used as a status symbol among commoners, as it comes in a combination of gold and black. Due to its high demand, depending on the region, it can be quite rare. In most superstitious groups the gemstone is assumed to repel undead and restless spirits.
Topaz | 500 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Comes in many colors, one of the most common ones, is light blue, however, the most prized one is a bright yellow also known as Imperial Topaz. Highly valued by nobility as they usually are offered as gifts at state gatherings. The gems are cut only by the most skilled master gem cutters.
Tourmaline | 100 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    It is one of the most prized minerals, and its fine crystals can be among the most beautiful examples in the mineral kingdom. Instead of being faceted, many fine Tourmaline crystals are preserved for their beauty. It is also believed that they can absorb any spell cast upon them, and convert it into lightning.
Turquoise | 10 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Carved or used as ornaments, turquoise has been considered a precious gem throughout history. Found in old tombs in the form of small animal statuettes or as ornaments on doors for royalty. It is a common good luck charm, used by horsemen and traders while traveling to ward off any ambushes or missteps in their future travels.
Variscite | 10 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Variscite, resembling Jade in color, is commonly used as a decorative element in clothing. According to belief, it possesses toxic properties harmful to nocturnal creatures if introduced through wounds or ingestion.
Violet Garnet | 750 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Or Rhodolite is mainly used by whitesmiths, jewelers, gaffers, and sometimes even blacksmiths for ornamentation purposes. In some cultures and faiths, garnets were thought to be the solidified blood of the religion's specific divine avatar.
Water Opal | 1000 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Water Opal, sometimes referred to as jelly opal, is a precious opal known for its transparent appearance and absence of body color. Unlike typical opals, water opals have a unique property of seemingly dissipating arcane spells.
White Pearl | 100 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    These specific Pearls are generally found in saltwater and are mostly sought after by royalty and nobility due to their rarity. The rarest ones, usually the size of someone's head are used in crystal balls, however, they mustn't have any imperfections.
Witherite | 50 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Witherite is a distinctive gemstone known for its requirement of a specific mount. Whether used in jewelry, armor, or any decorative item, it must be mounted on an electrum piece, a gold and silver alloy, otherwise, it risks becoming dislodged.
Wulfenite | 50 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Wulfenite is exceptionally soft and prone to breaking due to its flat, rectangular crystals. Despite this, its vibrant red and orange hues are captivating, leading gem cutters to produce small, sparkling, faceted gems ideal for settings with minimal handling, like inlays on furniture and royal displays.
Zircon | 50 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Zircon is a gemstone of many colors. There is also a transparent variant of it very similar to a diamond, which is why it's usually used as a cheaper alternative to it. Anyone without knowledge of gemstones can be easily fooled into believing that the stone is more expensive than it is.
Zoisite | 100 gp | 0.1 lb | loot
    This particular gem can be found in a variety of colors, including green, brown, pink, and black. These gems are particularly favored by people who lead secluded lives, as they are believed to ward off negative thoughts and emotions, whether they are caused by magic or other means. They are said to promote a sense of calm when worn. These gems are in high demand among hermits, monks, and common folk who live in remote areas.

== Raw Materials / Ore & Metals (66) ==

Adamantine | 500 gp | 2 lb | loot
    This amazingly hard metal is close to indestructible. It is known that this material can only be worked by world-renowned blacksmiths, as it is hard to work with, a regular blacksmith will simply destroy the ingot not being able to mold it into shape. Due to the blacksmith required, the cheapest price for an arrow can be anything in between 20 gold or 500 gold pieces, however, this is a stipulation as this ore is quite rare and hard to locate, and no one exactly knows their exact pricing. When the Adamantine is added to the weapon a +1 to its damage and attack roll can be added, however, this damage is not considered magical. If the Adamantine is added to armor or a shield, a +1 to the items AC is provided.
Alchemical Brass | 500 gp | 1 lb | loot
    On the rare occasions when Alchemists and Blacksmiths collaborate, they are able to create something truly unique. One such creation is an ingot that can be used to reinforce a weapon, making it so powerful that it can even break mountains when it strikes true. It is said that when Alchemical Brass is added to a regular weapon, it increases the weapon's accuracy and increases the chances of striking vital areas, making the strikes even more fatal.
Alchemical Silver | 500 gp | 2 lb | loot
    On the rare occasions when the Blacksmiths and the Alchemists get together, they manage to create an ingot that seems to look liquid but don't be fooled, this specific material is sturdier than the regular silver equipment. When it's supposed to be crafted, the Blacksmith and Alchemist work together to attach the liquid to a steel blade, and once attached it cannot be removed. If the Alchemical Silver weapon is used against a creature specifically stating that is resistant to nonmagical attacks that aren't silver, the maximum damage from the damage die is to be used. Ex: 8 + modifiers for an 1d8 + modifiers
Aluminum Ore | 5 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Aluminum Ore is a lightweight metal used as a material in alloys. Due to its soft nature, blacksmiths use it in combination with silver ore to craft cutlery or containers, while alchemists incorporate it into the creation of certain unique potions.
Antimony | 2 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Antimony is quite a bit of a brittle metal. Most lightning-attuned magical users use it as a conductor, a guide for untamed energy coursing through their spells. Alchemists grind antimony into a fine powder to extinguish fires, magical or otherwise. Rarely, the powdered form is used in pigments, valued for its distinctive, eye-catching sparkle.
Aurichalcum Ingot | 25 sp | 1 lb | loot
    When impurities are removed and clear ingots are produced, Aurichalcum Ingots finds several uses in essential components such as gears, ship parts, impellers, bearings, metallic sleeves, and other mechanical parts. Aurichalcum also remains tied to its ancient roots, with coins being minted to continue this long-standing tradition.
Aurichalcum Ore | 1 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Deep within the veins of the earth lies Aurichalcum, an alloy of zinc and copper, shimmering like brass yet valued as highly as gold. In the rarest of conditions, where pressure mounts and time stretches endlessly, this alloy crystallizes into Aurichalcite. Coins forged from Aurichalcum were considered treasures of great worth, symbolizing prosperity and power in societies long past.
Bismuth Bronze | 25 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Bismuth Bronze or Bismuth Brass is a copper alloy primarily used for manufacturing corrosion-resistant bolts, nails, and various other metal components. Its unique properties make it a favored choice for marine environments.
Brass | 75 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Brass is similar to bronze, another alloy containing Copper and Tin. Brass is quite a popular material for decoration due to its bright gold appearance. Its usage ranges from locks, hinges, and gears to plumbing and valves, as well as in the creation of instruments like horns or bells.
Bronze | 7 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Bronze, an alloy commonly created by mixing Copper and Tin, is the most familiar material for most artists, but also for treasurers for making coins. It is also broadly used in boat and ship fittings, as well as in the manufacturing process of mirrors, bells, and instrument strings.
Bronze Gunmetal | 15 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Bronze Gunmetal is an alloy of copper, tin, and zinc, with trace amounts of other metals such as lead or nickel, found its primary use in casting cannons, firearms, and various military equipment but also in fittings, gears, bearings, statues, and small objects such as buttons.
Carbon Steel | 1 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Commonly mistaken for dark steel due to its similar coloring but lacking its arcane properties, this alloy is a very strong variant of steel. However, it is more prone to rusting. It is primarily used to create cutting tools, springs, wrenches, hammers, and other types of tools used in construction.
Cerrosafe | 5 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Cerrosafe is a valuable material in the mold-making industry. Its ability to capture fine details with precision makes it a favorite choice for intricate ceramic and brick molds. Its low melting temperature also means it cannot withstand high heat applications, minimizing its use in processes like metal forging or high-temperature kiln and forge work.
Chromite Ingots | 2 sp | 0 lb | loot
    After the ore is smelted into Chromite Ingots, it is usually used to create sturdy tools for extreme climates, whether natural or more magical in nature. Alchemists occasionally use the purified metal smelted from the ore as a catalyst in brewing potions that provide resistance to the elements.
Chromite Ore | 3 ep | 1 lb | loot
    The Chromite Ore is used primarily in the manufacturing of high-quality steel, sometimes as a replacement for iron but more often combined with it. Depending on the amount used, it can provide metals with beautiful hues of green and red, making the wearers of armor or weaponry manufactured with it quite pleasing to the eye.
Chromite Pigment | 8 gp | 1 lb | loot
    When the Ore is smelted, the fine powder that remains behind is used for glassware as a pigment. Most religious buildings use it on their windows for intricate multi-hued hues and glass murals, representing scenes or deities. It also has some military applications, specifically in camouflage, when made into glass tower shields. If wielded properly, the light reflection off these shields can make the wearer nearly invisible.
Coal | 5 cp | 1 lb | loot
    "It is what I would call the Blacksmith's favorite type of ore. Without good quality coal, they will not be able to do what they do. On a small blacksmithing station you can get away with just some coal and wood, but when it comes to foundries, coal in high quantities is a must." The favorite tool of the metalworker, coal can be found mainly underground, making it one of the most common items uncovered while mining. Coal can be found close to the surface, as well, as underground, making dwarfs one of the most prevalent exporters.
Cobalt | 22 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Cobalt is one of the most mistaken ores for a gemstone, due to it’s shape and luster. Some naval fleets prefer cobalt-plated armor for their ships, as it resists the corrosive effects of saltwater far better than any other metal. Some alchemists utilize it for its conductive properties, and it is believed in fringe circles, that containers made from cobalt can store lightning, but these rumors remain unfounded.
Cold Iron | 500 gp | 2 lb | loot
    This specific iron, found in the most secluded deep mines quite cold to the touch, it is known that for those who venture into the Fey realm, it is a must. Most sylvan creatures damaged or attempting to attack a creature wearing Cold Iron armors seem to be taking an additional 1d4 cold damage.
Copper Ingot | 5 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Copper Ingot, once purified in the smelter, are used in the production of household utensils, weapons, armor, agricultural tools, and furniture ornamentation. They are often used by rookie blacksmiths as a training metal since their lower melting point reduces fuel consumption compared to many sturdier metals.
Copper Ore | 2 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Copper ore is commonly found in multiple veins alongside a variety of other metals. It is easily identified by its reddish-brown color. It serves as the foundation for multiple alloys, such as brass or bronze, and is also an important metal in coin minting in all regions. Arcane casters experiment with copper metal rods in enchanted devices, believing it enhances the flow of arcane energy.
Copper Slag | 5 cp | 1 lb | loot
    When the Ore is smelted, the fine powder that remains behind is used for glassware as a pigment. Most religious buildings use it on their windows for intricate multi-hued hues and glass murals, representing scenes or deities. It also has some military applications, specifically in camouflage, when made into glass tower shields. If wielded properly, the light reflection off these shields can make the wearer nearly invisible.
Crucible Steel | 750 gp | 1 lb | loot
    When blacksmiths get access to ancient magic, the forge becomes a sacred place. Here the finest weapons and armor are crafted. The most prized of these metals is crucible steel, which is known for its strength and durability. In times long gone it was said that crucible steel was forged in the fiery depths of the earth, tempered by the heat of a thousand suns. It was so strong that it could hold its edge even against the sharpest of blades. The finest blacksmiths would spend hours, if not days, working the crucible steel, carefully shaping it into the desired form. The result was a weapon or piece of armor that was virtually indestructible.
Daliumite | 1500 gp | 1 lb | loot
    It is known for its shimmering, golden appearance, often confused with gold, and is highly prized by blacksmiths. They usually wait months, if not years, for only small amounts of the ore, however, when they manage to obtain it, they will always turn a profit. When worked into bars, its surface is covered in intricate, swirling patterns that seem to change and shift as the ingot is moved. When held in the hand, daliumite feels warm to the touch and seems to have a faint vibration.
Duralumin | 5 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Duralumin, a durable and lightweight aluminum alloy, is employed in the most advanced territories for flight-related applications. Only a select few master blacksmiths can create and mold this alloy. It is also used to make armor for creatures that can fly.
Electrum | 30 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Sometimes referred to as Green Gold if manufactured, or White Gold, if it is a naturally occurring alloy. Primarily composed of Gold and Silver. In most cities, the balance between gold and silver is roughly 50-50 for the Electrum coin or bars manufacturing, however, in the more remote areas a higher percentage of silver is used. Due to this, most regions have stopped using Electrum coins altogether, as they match the big cities' manufacturing style.
Ferrochrome Aquarum | 5 cp | 1 lb | loot
    A very rare alloy, forgotten by time, with unknown schematics and properties. Its strength is as durable as iron when molded into shape, yet when the metal is moved, small ripples similar to rain splatter waves appear on it. Alchemists and casters who tried to identify it have been baffled, leading to dangerous consequences. Yet it is not magical in nature. A common, silent agreement among alchemists is that when adventurers ask for identification, they simply describe it as a hardened liquid metal.
Fool's Gold | 1 sp | 1 lb | loot
    "A few decades ago, there was a quartz mine controlled by its local Thieves’ Guild, at a certain point it struck a huge vein of Iron Pyrite, also known as Fool's Gold. Initially, like everyone else, they did believe it to be gold, but a quick assessment by the blacksmith from the area revealed it wasn’t. Unfortunately, that blacksmith was never heard from again. Fast-forward a couple of years, The Thieves Guild manages to replace all jewelry and all coins in the region. Eventually, it reached the lordship, and he sent a battalion to the town... I'm unsure if it still exists, as neither the battalion that was sent nor a word of the Thieves’ Guild ever came from that region." Iron Pyrite also known as the Fool's Gold, has limited uses, it is mainly used as a form of ignition in firearms, creating a small spark to ignite the gunpowder. It can also be used with flint as a method of starting fires.
Gold Ingot | 50 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Gold Ingots serve as the most common currency among merchants and nobles, valued for their purity and ease of transport. Jewelers also look for them as the metal is without impurities and is preferable to make bands, bracelets, or necklaces encrusted with gems. Some kingdoms even use the ingots to secure treaties and fund military campaigns, ensuring their armies remain well-equipped and loyal.
Gold Ore | 30 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Gold Ore is usually found by using two distinct methods. Miners gather it through tunneling deep into the earth, using pickaxes, explosives, or many other techniques. Or, in riverbeds. Prospectors pan for gold, sifting through sediment and rocks of the river to uncover precious flakes and nuggets.
Iron Ingot | 1 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Iron ingots can be used by skilled blacksmiths to craft nails, corner wedges, locks, flint, iron swords, manacles, shovels, pickaxes, and axes. They also produce iron beams, brackets, and supports to reinforce buildings and bridges, ensuring their longevity. Craftsmen use iron in furniture manufacturing, and on some rare occassion it is incorporated in jewlery.
Iron Ore | 6 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Iron Ore is one of the more abundant ores that can be found in both underground and mountainous regions. The quality of the iron can vary wildly depending on where it is located. Most of the iron gets transformed into pig iron which is the main material that goes into the forges to manufacture steel. Iron ore is a vital trade good, even more so than gold, with kingdoms leveraging their deposits to dominate commerce.
Lead | 3 sp | 1 lb | loot
    "The weirdest stuff I’ve heard of it being used for is, funny enough, make-up, but I feel it can have better usage. Most cities that have any plumbing have it thanks to lead." This specific metal is quite heavy and has a silvery appearance with a hint of blue. It's mainly used in ammunition due to it being heavy and resistant, plumbing and pipelines, as well as in fishing.
Lead Ingot | 3 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Through the realms, the lead ingot is a valuable commodity. Skilled craftsmen seek it out to make powerful bullets for their weapons. These bullets are then powered by magic so they can inflict a lot of damage on magical beasts or incorporeal beings. They are also used by alchemists to create potions and elixirs. The lead ingot is very valuable and versatile.
Liquid Solid Slate | 350 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Little is known about the process of liquefaction, however, when used as an alloy with another metal, it is said to be unbreakable. When struck by a weapon, an impenetrable shock-wave is created, pushing the weapon back. Due to its weight, it makes it difficult for people trying to move stealthily.
Manganese Brick | 15 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Manganese Bricks are not only used for their destructive flames but also for psychological warfare. When launched into enemy buildings and walls, they create intense heat and thick smoke, forcing defenders out of their strongholds. The horrible choking stench, coming from the charred brick, can linger for many hours, making it nearly impossible to reclaim the area.
Manganese Chips | 7 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Manganese Chips, after being mined, are rarely used by alchemists or herbalists in the production of potions or medicine, as well as in the creation of herbicides and brown paint. Their primary use is in the forging of a variety of important alloys to make them more resilient.
Mercury / Cinnabar | 25 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Mercury is found in large quantities within cinnabar deposits and generally found in areas associated with volcanic activity and hot springs. This silvery-looking metal changes into liquid form at room temperature, making it unique among its metallic counterparts. Mercury is primarily used by alchemists to treat skin ailments, though its effects remain a topic of debate.
Mithral | 500 gp | 2 lb | loot
    The rarest metal, provides an otherworldy calming blue glow, making it pleasant to the eyes. The equipment made out of this specific ore is sought after by wealthy nobility, as it is so light that even the poorest of fighters have increased mobility in it making them look better at sparring than they are, also providing a point of their statute to the common folk. While wearing armor crafted from this material, the Dexterity modifier increases by 1, the Strenght requirement decreases by 1 and its weight is halved.
Monel | 5 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Monel is created by combining nickel and copper, and it has minimal iron content. It boasts exceptional corrosion resistance, making it suitable for a wide range of applications. Whether it's for dog tags, piping, metal sheets, or nails used in saltwater ships, objects made from this alloy typically exhibit extended durability.
Nickel Ingot | 5 gp | 1 lb | loot
    A nickel ingot is a small, perfectly formed bar of pure nickel. It is said to be incredibly dense and heavy, and can only be forged by the most skilled blacksmiths. The metal has a shiny, silver color that glimmers in the light, and is an incredibly resilient metal, able to withstand even the strongest blows. Many tales and legends surround this rare and valuable metal, with some claiming that it holds magical properties and can bestow objects with incredible strength and durability, however in most regions it’s main purpose is for the creation of multiple types of coinage.
Nickel Ore | 5 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Nickel ore is a rare and prized mineral that can only be found deep within the earth. It is said to be incredibly strong and durable, making it highly demanded metal by blacksmiths and craftsmen alike.
Obsidian Ingot | 500 gp | 1 lb | loot
    An obsidian ingot is a small, expertly chiseled bar of pure obsidian. It is said to be incredibly dense and heavy, and can only be crafted by the most skilled blacksmiths. The metal has a deep, dark color that seems to absorb light, but when struck, it glows with a fiery inner light.
Palladium | 1000 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Palladium, with its lustrous white appearance, stands as a precious metal highly prized for its rarity and distinctive characteristics. Its notable usage in jewelry, especially as an alloy for crafting white gold.
Pewter | 2 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Pewter, a tin-based alloy that includes copper, antimony, and sometimes lead, is a popular material among blacksmiths for crafting tableware such as plates, cups, and utensils but also for decorative objects, mainly collectible statuettes and figurines, game figures, ship and other models, (replica) coins, pendants, plated jewellery and so on.
Pig Iron | 75 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Pig iron, commonly referred to as crude iron, is an intermediate product used by the blacksmiths in the production of steel. When combined with charcoal, through careful refining, impurities are removed, and the resulting steel becomes an amazing material for tools, construction, and warfare.
Platinum Ingot | 500 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Platinum ingots are extremely difficult to manufacture, requiring the hottest smelters fueled by lava or arcane fire to properly melt and refine platinum ore. Once purified into ingot form, only the most skilled craftsmen and elite workshops use it for the creation of high-quality items. However, the ingots are traded mainly as a commodity for high-value goods.
Platinum Ore | 30 pp | 1 lb | loot
    Platinum Ore is an extremely rare metal, found exclusively through underground mining. It possesses remarkable resistance to corrosion, even under high-temperature smelting, making it one of the most noble metals. Platinum is primarily used to create alloys with gold by jewelers for making intricate rings and necklaces sold at a quite high premium price.
Pure-Metal Brass Ingot | 75 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Pure-Metal Brass Ingots are manufactured from the finest curated copper and zinc. They have become increasingly popular for crafting religious objects such as candlesticks, cathedral bells, and reliquaries, often fetching a high price. Though slightly softer than bronze, their corrosion resistance makes it ideal for naval and coastal and naval military gear.
Pure-Metal Bronze Ingot | 75 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Pure-Metal Bronze Ingots are made from an alloy of the most refined chunks of copper and tin, in equal amounts. Skilled blacksmiths at prestigious smelters refine the metals through precise smelting techniques, ensuring that every ingot maintains a uniform composition. Without impurities or flaws, these blacksmiths can forge higher than normal quality tools, weapons, armor, and building materials such as decorative tiles for flooring or detailed sculptures.
Rhenium | 3 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Rhenium, sometimes referred to as the "Alchemist's Metal," is primarily used in the making of alloys with tungsten. To experienced alchemists, Rhenium symbolizes the perfection of all matter physical, mental, and spiritual. In many potions, a single drop of liquid Rhenium is added to strengthen their potency.
Rose Gold | 25 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Rose gold, created by combining pure gold and copper, is popular in specialized jewelry. It is more malleable than pure gold, making it a preferred material for whitesmiths, and it is less expensive than gold or white gold.
Rutile Powder | 3 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Rutile when ground into a fine powder, it turns into a beautiful pigment, bringing color to canvases and ceramics alike. In the world of medicine, its strength and resistance to corrosion make it a primary material, protecting implants and ensuring their longevity.
Selenium | 1000 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Selenium is a rare and precious mineral that is highly sought after for its unique properties. It is said to have a deep, iridescent luster that shimmers with every color of the rainbow. When it is struck with a hammer or chisel, it gives off a pure, clear tone that is said to have a soothing effect on those who hear it. Despite its many wondrous properties, Selenium is also said to be incredibly difficult to obtain, and many brave and daring adventurers have set out in search of it, only to be met with failure or their demise.
Silver Ingot | 5 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Silver Ingots, once produced, have a multitude of uses and are sought after by craftsmen of all trades. They can be fashioned into boxes that preserve wine and food, cutlery, fine art, gemmed jewelry, and even masterfully crafted armor and weaponry. Depending on the region, silver can sometimes surpass even the most brilliant-cut diamonds in its price.
Silver Ore | 3 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Silver Ore is one of the most sought-after ores, valued for its use in metallurgy and coinage. It is commonly made as an alloy with other metals and is the primary material for minting the most circulated type of coin. Unlike gold, which is often hoarded by the elite, silver circulates widely, fueling trade across vast empires. It can also be ground into fine dust for use in the making of mirrors and weaponry, as it is known to hunt down creatures of the night.
Steel | 2 sp | 1 lb | loot
    "Well, the building block of life, or so they say. I do love its glimmer in the night, especially when guardsmen wear their polished armors, keeps me safe, you know? Created from slabs of iron ore in high heat furnaces with added charcoal, it creates one of the sturdiest metals for weaponry. Steel is used mainly in cutlery, inexpensive rings, armor, and weaponr
Tin | 4 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Tin is usually combined with Copper, due to it being soft, to prevent its oxidation. Doesn't have many uses besides the preservation of metals.
Titanium | 250 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Titanium is an incredible metal. It is really expensive and rare, but it also has some amazing strengthening properties that make it one of the strongest metals around! When forged into weapons and armor by the most famous blacksmiths throughout the realm, this precious silver-gray metal is said to be near indestructible, not to mention incredibly light yet stronger than steel, making it the perfect material for crafting weapons and armor.
Tombak | 5 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Tombak is an alloy of copper and zinc used to imitate gold in cheap jewelry and for gilding. It is a cheap malleable alloy mainly used for medals, ornament, decoration and other ornamental uses. Yet the most nefarious of folk tend to purchase it in bulk to sell it at a premium as gold.
Tungsten Ingot | 75 gp | 0 lb | loot
    Tungsten Ingots are a rare product, crafted only by experienced blacksmiths using some of the hottest smelters. The pure Tungsten Ingot’s primary use lies in alchemical research, particularly in the development of new, powerful acids capable of dissolving everything. Due to their rarity and the immense difficulty in forging them, locating a properly refined one is akin to finding a needle in a haystack.
Tungsten Rods | 300 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Tungsten, sometimes referred to as "Wolfram", is one of the heaviest metals used in any form of crafting. Due to its heavy nature and tough molding, it is primarily used in alloying with softer metals, such as Rhenium or Aluminum all to enhance their durability and shelf life. Another use of tungsten is in the creation of combat battering rams, from which it derives its name.
Vanadium | 500 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Vanadium is a desired metal throughout the realms, and for very good reason. It is insanely rare and has a deep, green-gray luster that shines in the light. Some blacksmiths and alchemists can imbue vanadium with magical properties, making it even more valuable. Vanadium is very light yet stronger than steel and is speculated that it can absorb and store magical energy.
White Gold | 75 cp | 1 lb | loot
    White gold, an alloy of gold and silver, should not be mistaken for platinum. Known as gray gold in some regions, it is highly valued by the nobility for its high price and pristine silver look in their jewelry.
Zamac | 3 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Zamac is a zinc-based alloy primarily used in jewelry, but it is also utilized for making tools, bolts, and hinges. It is particularly favored in applications where paint is used, as dyes adhere exceptionally well to it.
Zinc | 15 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Zinc is a rare, shimmering metal that is known for its ability to protect against the elements. It has a soft, silver color that glimmers in the light. It is often used to coat other metals, such as iron or steel, to protect them from rust and decay. In ancient times, it was believed to have magical properties and was used in the crafting of powerful talismans and amulets, even magical mirrors.

== Raw Materials / Stone & Masonry (25) ==

Alabaster | 2 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Alabaster's softness enables it to be carved readily into elaborate forms making it a popular material for carved sculpture. It is also often used for ornamental building work, such as for sculptures, vases, and small decorative carvings.
Basalt Stone | 5 sp | 1 lb | loot
    The basalt rock comes mainly in a dark-gray color and is used in construction for building blocks or in the groundwork, or cobblestones and in making statues by famous masons.
Chalk | 2 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Chalk is an earthy type of limestone, found mainly in light colors, and is highly sought after, as it can have multiple uses. It is used for writing on slate tables or leaving messages in the wild, to delimit terrain in field sports, in its powder form. It can also be used by tailors and other fabric workers to draw lines and patterns on leather or fabric, to help them cut and sew the material in a precise and accurate way. It is also used in scrying or to create magical symbols and spells on the ground or surfaces, such as walls or doors, to keep out unwanted intruders or to contain a summoned creature.
Chalk Limestone Ashlar | 1 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Created by the finest masons, tiles were crafted for the exterior stylization of buildings or, in rare cases, as floor tiles. Their light coloring made them a popular choice for the ornamentation of churches, cathedrals, or royal manors constructed from stone. Despite this, their limited durability as tiles led to their replacement with either marble or granite in more recent times.
Clay Brick | 2 sp | 1 lb | loot
    The clay was shaped into bricks by pushing it into a wooden mold, then heating it in kilns. They can also be glazed with colorful finishes or decorated with intricate patterns, making them a popular choice for decorative building materials.
Cobblestones | 1 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Rounded river stone or split quarry rubble, sorted by size and sold by the cartload for paving streets, yards, or for making cellars. While they are easy to be found and sold by the cartload, they have not much use otherwise.
Gabbro | 3 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Gabbro, known as black granite in construction terms, serves primarily in building stone fences due to its durability despite its challenging workability. It is mainly extracted for the other minerals found within the same rock, including chromium, nickel, cobalt, copper, gold, silver, and platinum.
Glass | 5 sp | 1 lb | loot
    The process of shaping glass is time-consuming, and to create it, glassblowers need a vast amount of skill. After the glassblowing process is completed and the glass is still hot, it is placed into molds to create bottles, vials, windows, and other glass objects such as vases or decorative objects.
Gneiss | 12 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Gneiss has many uses as a building material such as flooring, ornamental stones, gravestones, facing stones on buildings, and work surfaces. Depending on where is acquired, it can have beautiful patterns making it one of the most sought-after types of materials for ornamental construction.
Granite | 2 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Granite is a common rock that comes in various colors, such as white, dark-gray, and brown. Because of its range of colors, it's often cut into shapes for gravestones, memorials, and pavement. Its durability and low maintenance needs make it a suitable choice for these uses.
Laterite | 3 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Laterite is mainly used in making bricks, but it is used in all types of paint, primers, powders, and coatings, among other things. Used as a pigment in tiles, floor tiles, flooring products, ceramics, pottery, and many other items involving tiles and ceramics.
Marble | 1 gp | 1 lb | loot
    The favorite tool of skilled sculptors, marble, transforms thoughts and emotions into beautiful sculptures as they carve it. Polished to a shine, it earns the sculptor a hefty profit when sold. Marble is one of the heaviest and most resilient stones that can be bought with coin. Its elegance makes it a popular choice for nobility, who use it for indoor flooring and heavy furnishings.
Murum | 5 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Murum finds application in various heavy-duty tasks such as plinth filling, road pavements, backfilling in trenches, and footing pits. Its absence of organic matter and ease of compaction, leading to the formation of durable surfaces, make it an ideal soil for construction purposes.
Oolite | 10 gp | 0.02 lb | loot
    Oolites, often nicknamed Cave Pearls, are typically found in caves and vary in colors such as green, white, or brown, depending on the cave's specific biome. Their primary uses are decorative, used either as ornaments or crushed to create vivid inks.
Paradiso | 5 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Paradiso is a rare and distinct granite color, found only in a few locations throughout the realms. It is in high demand but short supply, often causing regional prices to skyrocket. Its rich purple hue gives it a premium appearance, making it an exquisite choice for indoor furniture such as countertops and tables.
Pietra Serena | 10 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Pietra Serena is a refined marble variety, known for its characteristic blue-gray tones. Other shades, including deep blue and black, also exist. Highly valued by artists, it is commonly used in sculptures and architectural embellishments such as pilasters, entablatures, and decorative elements.
Pumice | 1 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Light, porous stone formed from cooled volcanic froth, so full of air bubbles that a large piece floats on water. Scribes use it to smooth parchment, carpenters to polish wood, and bathhouses to scrub calluses off feet. It is gathered along shores near volcanoes, where after an eruption it washes up in such large quantities that whole beaches turn gray with it.
Quarry Rubble | 3 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Broken stone left over once the good blocks have been cut out, usually sold by the cartload for foundations, wall cores, and road bed, but also cellars. It is the cheapest thing a quarry sells and the reason a quarry can price its dressed stone as low as it does. Builders look at the a load that is sold, and judge its pricing on how little soil comes with it.
Quartzite | 5 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Quartzite is a decorative stone and may be used to cover walls, as roofing tiles, flooring, and stairsteps, due to its strength, it is also used to construct roads, bridges, and dams. When cut and polished, the rock is quite beautiful, as well as durable.
Quicklime | 8 cp | 1 lb | loot
    White powder made by burning limestone or seashells in a kiln until they crumble, and the main ingredient of mortar, plaster, and whitewash. Masons and tanners buy it by the sack, and farmers spread it to fertilized fields. Mixed with water it hisses and grows hot enough to scald, which is why lime is always carried in sealed casks and never through the rain.
Sandstone | 8 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Being a commonly used type of brick for roads and buildings, sandstone blocks are often sourced from quartz quarries. They can also be located rarely in copper mines. Stone masons favor sandstone due to its ease of cutting and durability against elements. For wall fortification, hollowed-out sandstone bricks are filled with dirt that are used as cushions against heavy fire from trebuchets or catapults. It is also cut in huge slabs, to be used as a pavement in more densely populated areas.
Siltstone | 5 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Siltstone, a type of mudrock, is made of compressed clay and silt. It absorbs water well, making it useful for aquifers and water storage but unsuitable for most construction due to its porous nature, and when it is, it is primarily used in areas where no other type of rock material is available.
Siltstone Slabs | 1 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Siltstone blocks are primarily used as dimension stones, where they can be cut into blocks or slabs for architectural purposes or for polishing certain metals, such as steel. In high-density areas, they are commonly used for indoor flooring, landscape decoration, and garden walls.
Slate | 1 sp | 1 lb | loot
    "Slate doesn't get the love it deserves. Most writing boards are made out of it, also, it is quite resistant if cut into shapes and made into shingles" Slate comes in many colors, from shades of gray to green to purple, even cyan depending on where it was harvested. It's mainly used in writing tablets and roofing.
Stone Brick | 4 sp | 1 lb | loot
    The stone brick is often expensive to produce, as it requires specialized tools and skilled craftsmen or masons to shape and polish, as well, and must be mined from deep within the earth or quarried from rock formations, making it often reserved for the most prestigious building projects, such as castles, walls, forts, and fortresses.

== Raw Materials / Timber & Lumber (37) ==

Aspen Log | 5 gp | 0 lb | loot
    Aspen wood is white, soft, lightweight, and fairly strong and has a multitude of uses. Mainly found in colder climates, making it one of the more widespread trees. Its bark is used in multiple medicinal poultices by herbalists mainly for its antioxidants or anti-inflammatory properties.
Aspen Lumber | 1 gp | 14 lb | loot
    Aspen Lumber is a wood that is soft but fairly strong, and it is closest to white in color. It has a fine-grained, uniform texture and lacks a distinctive pattern. Due to its light color, it is perfect for making paper, matchsticks, furniture, doors, and window frames.
Bamboo | 1 sp | 5 lb | loot
    Hollow, jointed cane that grows faster than any tree, and its roots can expanding for miles, cut in warm and wet regions and shipped in tied bundles. It is light yet strong enough for scaffolding, fences, fishing rods, and water pipes, while the thinner canes are made into flutes and writing brushes.
Birch Log | 17 cp | 0 lb | loot
    The Birch Log is a common yet essential item within the realms, prized for its versatility and utility. It is a thin, pale log harvested from the birch trees that grow in abundance throughout the land. The wood is strong yet pliable, making it perfect for a wide variety of uses. Craftsmen and artisans alike value the Birch Log for its ability to be shaped and molded into a wide range of objects. It is often used to create everything from simple tools and household items to intricate carvings and intricate pieces of furniture.
Birch Plank | 2 sp | 14 lb | loot
    The plank is incredibly strong and durable, yet surprisingly light and easy to work with. It is the perfect material for creating everything from delicate jewelry and intricate carvings to elegant furniture and powerful artifacts. Most towns' sawmills that trade in it, are speculated to have highly skilled craftsmen and artisans in their midst
Brushwood Bundle | 2 cp | 5 lb | loot
    Thin branches and prunings tied into a small bundle, the cheapest fuel there is and the one every baker and brickmaker burns by the ton. It gives a fast fire that dies quickly, which is great for an oven far better than it suits a hearth. Woodsmen make them out of what is left once the useful timber for planks is cut.
Cedar Log | 14 cp | 0 lb | loot
    It is typically harvested from cedar trees that grow in forested areas and is known for its durability and resistance to decay. Cedar logs are often used to create strong and sturdy structures, such as buildings, bridges, and fences, as well as furniture and other wooden objects.
Cedar Plank | 2 sp | 14 lb | loot
    Because cedar wood is strong and durable, it is an ideal material for creating frames and other structural components of a building. Cedar planks are often used for flooring, siding, roofing, and other applications where strength and stability are important.
Charcoal | 5 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Wood burned slowly under a covering of earth until black chunks remain. The end producut that comes out of that is Charcoal. It burns far hotter and cleaner than the wood ever did. Smiths, glassblowers, and metalworkers buy it by the cartload from the colliers, who live in the forest for weeks at a time tending their smoking mounds.
Darkwood | 500 gp | 2 lb | loot
    The favorite wood type of most sentient woodland beings. This wood is extremely light, any items manufactured with it will only weigh half of their original counterpart. As this wood is quite flexible, on most items that are made partially of wood, such as a battleax or a mace, it cannot be used as it will eventually splinter and break.
Fir Log | 2 sp | 0 lb | loot
    The Fir Log is a sturdy and versatile piece of wood, prized for its strength and durability. The logs are cut from the resilient fir trees that grow in the snowy mountains, imbued with their rugged spirit and strength. Because fir wood is strong and durable, it is an ideal material for creating frames and other structural components of a building. In addition to their practical uses, fir planks are also prized for their attractive appearance. The wood has a distinctive light-colored, straight-grain appearance, making it a popular choice for decorative purposes.
Fir Plank | 2 sp | 0 lb | loot
    The Fir Plank is a common and versatile building material, used throughout the realms for a wide variety of purposes. It is harvested from tall, straight fir trees that grow in the cool, misty forests of the northern lands. The wood is strong and durable, yet light and easy to work with.
Hickory Log | 15 cp | 0 lb | loot
    Hickory logs are used in a variety of applications, including the manufacture of furniture, by the most skilled carpenters, flooring, and tool handles. It is also commonly used as a fuel for cooking and smoking meats, as it imparts a very distinctive flavor.
Hickory Plank | 2 sp | 16 lb | loot
    This specific light-colored wood is widely used for tools like hammers, picks, axes, wheels, ladders, flooring, and furniture.
Ironwood Log | 4 sp | 0 lb | loot
    "We call it Ironwood, but it usually has its own name depending on the region. The folks at the lumber mills hate this type of wood, as almost every time they have to replace their main blade. Not to mention if anyone wants lumber from it they need to have a proper amount of gold too. It is very resilient!" Ironwood comes in several colors, ranging from red to gray to bright browns, depending on the region where it was harvested. It is mainly used as firewood as it burns very slowly. When used in furniture, it is usually only used for small decorative add-ons, as most woodworker tools are not efficient in carving it.
Mahogany Log | 250 gp | 14 lb | loot
    Mahogany is a straight-grained, reddish-brown timber that is mainly harvested for the wood, found in most warmer climates, mainly tropical forests, and extremely rare. Some territories, have this specific wood outright banned for import as it tends to put a high economic pressure on the territory as it is also in high demand.
Mahogany Lumber | 50 gp | 14 lb | loot
    Mahogany is important lumber prized for its beauty, durability, and color, and tends to be the most prized type of lumber. It is used for paneling and to make furniture, boats, decorative pieces, musical instruments, and other items.
Maple Log | 17 cp | 0 lb | loot
    Maple trees are usually in high demand in the spice industry because of their sap, syrup, and sugar can be made.
Maple Lumber | 3 sp | 0 lb | loot
    The lumber is mainly used to make furniture and is the best choice for wooden flooring.
Oak Log | 2 sp | 0 lb | loot
    The logs are usually processed into lumber, however, it's also commonly used in bakeries, furnaces, or home fireplaces as kindle.
Oak Plank | 25 cp | 14 lb | loot

Palo Verde | 25 gp | 0 lb | loot
    The Palo Verde tree can be easily recognized by its green-colored bark. This tree is typically harvested for its bark, which can be used for multiple purposes. The inner bark can be woven into rope or baskets, or used for making chair bottoms. Additionally, it is utilized for making canoes, and clothing, and as a surface for paintings and maps. The bark can also be used as shingles and wall coverings.

Pine Tar | 1 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Thick, black, sticky tar cooked out of pine stumps and roots in slow-burning kilns, and sold in barrels and iron pots. Primarely used by shipwrights who caulk hulls with it. Sailors are covered in it so often that in many harbor towns a sailor is simply called a tar.
Poplar Log | 5 gp | 0 lb | loot
    Poplar is one of the most available types of wood. Due to its lower price due to its accessibility, it is an excellent choice for most beginners to intermediate carpenters. It also has a wide range of uses, such as construction, joinery, or carving.
Poplar Lumber | 3 sp | 14 lb | loot
    It is perfect for items like pallets, boxes, and crates, but also, artisan bowls, wooden plates, and other fine carpentry. Due to its relatively modest price point, poplar is a great choice for commercial uses. However, it can dent or scratch easily, making it undesirable for high-wear items, as well as, over time its color fades to a medium brown within months.
Redwood Log | 3 sp | 0 lb | loot

Retama | 25 gp | 0 lb | loot

Sandalwood Log | 125 gp | 0 lb | loot
    Sandalwood is primarily harvested for its oil. The Sandalwood forests are considered one of the most expensive woods within the realms. From the oil, a lot can be crafted such as perfumes, soaps, candles, and incense, as well as, if turned into a fine powder, for medicine, religious or cultural purposes, also, as a flavor for foods and beverages.
Sandalwood Lumber | 25 gp | 2 lb | loot
    Sandalwood is extremely rare and precious, it is used for intricate carvings, to make sturdy furniture, medicines, cosmetics, and religious rites. The heartwood of the tree is treasured for its aroma and is one of the finest natural materials for making beautifully carved furniture and tools.
Teak Log | 25 cp | 0 lb | loot

The logs are often used to create strong and sturdy structures, such as buildings, bridges, and fences, as well as furniture. The wood is also prized for its resistance to pests and insects, making it a popular choice for use in household items.
Teak Plank | 3 sp | 14 lb | loot
    Because Teak wood is resistant to rot and decay, as well as insects and other pests, it is a popular choice for outdoor applications, such as decks, patios, and fencing. Teak can also be used in the construction of ships and other watercraft, as it is resistant to water damage and warping.
Vertugal | 4 sp | 0 lb | loot
    "Some say that the forest spirits don't like it when you cut these trees. As time has passed, more and more sightings of these beings have been made, taking the shape of different woodland creatures, from cats to foxes, deer to bears. If you notice one and you plan to bring harm to the forest, you won't be in this realm for long." A type of soft, light-colored wood with a straight grain is commonly used in pulpwood and paper production. This wood is popular in the printing industry, as it is lightweight and easy to work with. It can also be used in combination with fabric, such as cotton, or animal leather, to create parchment or other paper products.
Walnut Log | 100 gp | 0 lb | loot
    Walnut wood is commonly used for furniture, flooring, cabinetry, and decorative items due to its rich color and workability. The leaves can be used for sunburns, itching as well, and dandruff, when processed by the herbalists in poultrices.

    Once harvested, walnut lumber is commonly used for furniture, flooring, cabinetry, and decorative items due to its rich color and workability. and due to it being a low price, it is in high demand by most carpenters and woodworkers.

== Seasonal & Themed / Red Harvest (27) ==

Blood Potion | 1 sp | 1 lb | consumable
    Every year in the shadowed weeks of the Red Harvest, when the fields lie fallow and folk light lanterns carved from turnips, you’ll find barrels of blood potion hauled out from cellars and cellared caves. Despite its name, there’s not a drop of true blood in the drink. It’s a thick, jewel-red brew made from cherries or dark forest berries, boiled down into a syrupy kompot and spiced with cloves, cinnamon bark, and sometimes a splash of vinegar to give it a bite sharp enough to wake the dead.
Box Of Horrors | 2 gp | 1 lb | container
    They appear only during Red Harvest, when the nights grow long and the air stinks of smoke and cider. Children wait all year for them, as they are filled to the brim with sweets, darting between bonfires with sticky fingers and purple-stained lips. Grown traders pretend they’re above such things, though I’ve seen more than one caravan master “inspect” an entire box before camp is set.
Brain | 5 cp | 2 lb | loot
    They’re said to come from… well, that’s the tricky part. A few merchants claim they’re harvested after accidents or battles, yet others swear it’s some artisan’s grisly craft, pressed and preserved with secret oils. I can’t say which is true, but either way, they’re fragile. Keep it away from sunlight and rough hands.
Claw Cup | 1 sp | 1 lb | loot
    I’ve drunk from many strange vessels in my years, horns carved from aurochs, clay mugs still warm from the kiln, even a polished turtle shell on the coast, but nothing feels quite like wrapping your hand around a Claw-Cup at Red Harvest. Picture a goblet where the stem is forged iron, darkened and rough as old blood, and shaped into a talon. Its black claws grip a bowl of smoky glass as if it’s holding on for dear life. Some smiths even add scratches and nicks so it looks as though the claw fought for it.
Creamatory | 2 sp | 1 lb | consumable
    There’s a saying on the market, if the cream leaves a perfect spiral after your first sip, your year will end in fortune. If it curdles instead, best not travel far from home, as bad luck awaits. A long time ago, I had ignored that superstition, laughed it off, even mocked it, and ended up breaking an cart axle in the dark swamps, and got stranded for a few days 'till, it got fixed. Since then, I always watch the cream's shape. Call me foolish, but when you’ve seen cream clump into a shapes only on that specific evening, you learn to give the drink its respect.
Crow Feathers | 5 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    The crow is a clever creature, when making it, most say it's a watcher and a guide, a creature that knows the secrets of both the field and the midden. Binding two of its feathers is said to capture a small sliver of that cunning, and for the red string tied to its base? That acts as a focus. It binds the magic, so to speak, keeps it from wandering off. This specific suppersticion is divided as for every soul who sees it as a lucky charm, there’s another who’ll spit on the ground and call it a fool’s gamble.
Dark Rose | 3 sp | 0.1 lb | loot
    You won't find this harvested and cared for in any proper garden, and thank the stars for that. I had only ever seen it in ashy soil of old forgotten battlefields or in the silent, dark and thick forests where the very air feels damp, old and watchful. Its stems and leaves are the deep purple of a day old bruise, veined with a crimson so dark it’s nearly black, while the flower itself, does resemble blood in color. It has no scent, instead, it carries a metallic smell, like dread on the wind, and the melancholy decay of the forgotten.
Darkness Eye | 15 sp | 0.5 lb | loot
    The first time I saw a Darkness Eye, it was in the cracked palm of a street vendor in Marrowgate, and I swear the thing looked like it was swallowing the light whole. A gem, indeed, but not one that glitters or winks at you, with it's jumping colors. Its surface is dull and black as pitch, yet somehow deeper than any pit. Stare too long, and your stomach tightens as though you’re falling into it. Some say it’s not stone at all, but a fragment of night itself created into a round shape.
Fangs Necklace | 1 gp | 0.5 lb | loot
    Ah, the Fangs Necklace. Every year, just before the Red Harvest fires are lit, jewelers from Dockhaven to the hill markets of Bristlethorn start cutting these out by the dozen. It’s a mostly dramatic piece, for decoration. Quite sharp, toothlike pendants dangling from a wire or chain, usually fashioned from red glass or smoky quartz. Some gemmasters will cut actual garnets into points, but most of what you’ll see glittering in the market stalls is mostly clever work with colored glass. When worn in torchlight, the pieces catch a red glimmer that looks unsettlingly like fresh blood, aimed to resemble the night fangs.
Finger Claw | 3 sp | 0.5 lb | loot
    They’re not weapons, despite the way a few swaggering lads like to scratch the air with them. They’re costume, pure and simple, but folk treat them with a strange seriousness. In the riverlands, berry stained claws are handed out to children to mark the turning of the season, while in the port towns you’ll find claws inlaid with bits of colored glass, sold at a premium to visiting merchants who want to blend in with the eccentric nobility.
Gravestone | 5 sp | 25 lb | loot
    Gravestones as when it comes with Red Harvest's nature, oddly enough, aren't just decorative. I’ve found that wherever these grave markers are set, the ground beneath has a curious habit of stirring, like it is calling. More than one caretaker in Bacau's Letea District swears that a cheap plaster marker is just as likely to hide a restless corpse as the real thing. Some scholars argue it’s all coincidence or that the Red Harvest festival simply draws out the necromantic residue that lingers in cities from the countless wars.
Heart | 1 sp | 0.5 lb | loot
    Looks like a stage prop, doesn’t it? Too shiny, too perfect, the sort of thing you’d expect dangling from a street conjurer’s sleeve. Yet hold one in your palm and you’ll swear it thrums against your skin, slow, steady, like the echo of your own chest. I’ve seen hardened mercenaries drop the thing lika a curse, as if they’d grabbed a live rat.
Lantern Jack | 1 sp | 5 lb | loot
    Ah, the Lantern Jack. No market cart nor tavern stoop feels quite right during Red Harvest without one grinning back at you. At its simplest, it’s just a hollowed pumpkin with eyes and a mouth carved in, a candle guttering inside. Yet there’s something more to them than vegetables and wax. Stand in a village square at dusk, the chill coming down, and those rows of leering faces look near alive, like a crowd watching you from the dark.
Mimic Sandwich | 2 sp | 0.5 lb | consumable
    The first time I saw one of these was, I think, in a riverside tavern in Vaslui during Red Harvest, I nearly spilled my ale laughing. Picture this, a sandwich with boiled eggs glaring up at you, stuck with sticks of celery on top, green and black olives set in the whites to give the “eyes” a hungry gleam. The tavernkeep had draped a slice of cucumber out the side like a lolling tongue, and the cheese, melted and oozed over the bread like crooked teeth. It’s meant to resemble a mimic, of course, and after a few tankards, when the room starts spinning, it’ll start looking unnervingly alive.
Monkey Paw | 0 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Monkey Paw... this paw grants wishes. Each finger stands for one, and once a wish is spent, the finger locks tight, stiff as bone. It gives you what you asked for, aye, but it takes the spirit of your words and twists it like a rope. I knew a caravan master who begged for endless gold. He got it, but it rained from the skies as molten coins, burning his camp to ash. By the time you’re on the last finger, most folk would sooner beg for death than ask another boon.
Monster Pie | 2 sp | 0.5 lb | consumable
    Don’t let the name fool you into thinking it’s something grisly like ogre or troll kidney. No, it’s just a pastry, and a festive one at that. A golden crust, brushed with butter till it gleams, filled to bursting with red berries. Cooks and bakers slice crude eyes and a jagged mouth into the crust so it grins at you with wicked cheer. Some faces come out goofy, some downright ghastly, and I swear the best tasting ones look like they’re about to bite back.
Murder Cake | 3 sp | 2 lb | consumable
    These I first encountered this cake at a masquerade in Sibiu, during Red Harvest, where the nobility have a taste for games that would curl the toes of a honest fisher. A Murder Cake is no simple dessert, it's the centerpiece of a theatrical evening where guests play at being detectives and killers. The cake itself is a grand, multi-tiered affair, often dark as a starless night with frosting the colour of old blood or fashioned to look like a gothic manor, but it also can be as simple as, a layer of cake with jam and a knife sticking out of it.
Purple Vampire Crab | 2 gp | 2 lb | consumable
    A an amazing thing I have heard, from an old delta crabber, is that the intensity of the purple on their shells predicts the severity of the coming winter. The deeper the purple, the harder the frosts. He also swore that if you keep one in a tank, it will only thrive if you add a pinch of silt from its home delta each month. He called it "homesickness," and I think he might be right.
Sanguine Cocktail | 3 sp | 1 lb | consumable
    I first saw this in The Gilded Sea Horse, a tavern tucked away in a hidden back alley close to the docks, where everything smells of rot and old salt. Matel, the tavern owner, pushed the glass across the counter with the largest smirk her face could muster. In the guttering light it looks like fresh blood, a deep, viscous red that clings to the glass and runs slow enough to make you anxious. My heart skipped, then my tongue said other things. It’s sweet at first, like honeyed brambles warmed in your palm, then a pinch of lemon snaps through so you wake up. Underneath it all you get the clean bite of a highland gin distilled up in the cold hills behind Iteshti.
Screamsickle Shake | 2 sp | 1 lb | consumable
    A small superstition clings to the Screamsicle Shake, young children are told that if you spill a drop on the wooden floor, will make next year crop fail. I have a feeling this was invented by tired mothers who didn’t want sticky footprints on their clean floors. Still, I never dared spill my own cup at my mother's house, and I’m not about to test the spirits patience now.
Shapeshifter | 2 sp | 1 lb | consumable
    Traders like me see jars of apple jam moving by the crate in early autumn, traded from orchard stewards to dairy farms and back again, all so this one concoction can be made for a handful of nights. It doesn’t hold very well, by following morning it clots and sours, which is why no one ships it far, one of it's reasons that it got its weird name, but kids love it.
Skull Candle | 1 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Tavern-keeps and innholders are their primary patrons. They are cheap to buy and cheaper to maintain, and they give a place a certain... character. I also must say, they are particularly fond of them, claiming the grim visage keeps rowdy dockhands in their seats. I find they are more often used as a target for throwing knives after the seventh round of ale, but for one night a year, I think the keeps can manage.
Skull Ring | 15 cp | 1 lb | loot
    While a staple for the Red Harvest, wear one long enough and folk assume you’ve done something grim, as I forgot to take it of for a few. I wore one for a fortnight on the in a runty village while traveling, just to see, and the dockhands and farmers alike, gave me a wide berth, muttering that I must’ve lost a crew to the deep, or that something horrible has happened to me.
Spirit Lights | 0 cp | 0 lb | loot
    Well now's the Spirit Lights, and encounter that I don't wish. I’ve seen them twice in my travels, and that’s twice too many if you ask me. They look harmless at first, little flickers drifting through the dusk, not larger than fireflies. But the veterans of the harvest know better. Those lights don’t flit about all year. They only show themselves during the Red Harvest, when the moons line up and the fields glow crimson under their light. Stay out in the rows too long chasing one, and something follows. I’ve heard the screams in the night, thin and distant.
Stitch-Me-Up Pie | 2 sp | 1 lb | consumable
    These pies show up most often in autumn markets, when bakers want to flaunt their skill as much as their orchard’s bounty. The more intricate the “stitching,” the higher the price. In my travels, a while back, I once saw a baker take commissions from adventuring companies, shaping pies with gashes that matched their battle scars. Silly? Absolutely. But the guildsmen paid handsomely, and everyone left sticky-fingered and grinning.
Witches Brew | 2 sp | 1 lb | consumable
    Younglings adore it, and even some of the older folk take a mug for nostalgia, sometimes with a drop of spiced brandy slipped in by a cheeky tavern keeper. There are stories tied to it. Some say if you stir your cup thrice under the harvest moon you’ll glimpse the face of your future love in the foam. I tried once in Harthen Hollow. All I got was a sticky nose and a laugh from the brewer’s daughter who had been watching me the whole time. Maybe that counts as magic enough.
Zombie | 2 sp | 1 lb | consumable
    Its nature is one of pure, simple celebration. You will find it most often during the harvest months when the squash are fat and the berry bushes are heavy. It is not a drink of nobility or ceremony, but of hearth and home. It is made by parents and grandparents for their little ones, a special treat to mark the end of a hard season’s work. It has no kick to it, mind you, which is the entire point. It lets the children feel they are part of the grown-ups' festivities, clinking their own mugs together.

== Tools & Craft / Carpentry (38) ==

Archery Target | 2 gp | 25 lb | loot
    Originally, archery targets were simple mounds of earth, but skilled archers quickly wore them down by hitting the same spots repeatedly. To solve this, wooden archery targets were developed, made primarily of hardwood and reinforced with heavy rope and straw to handle the high amount of arrows. Wealthier archers could afford targets with colored paper, marked in red, blue, and black to create the famous bullseye design.
Ballista | 100 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Ballista is a weapon that is used mainly for fort and settlement defense. It relies on different mechanics than a regular crossbow, using two levers with torsion springs instead of a tension prod. There are some rumors that a more advanced weapon exists called a polybolos, however, these rumors remain unfounded.
Barrel | 2 sp | 3 lb | loot
    Barrels are among the most widely used storage containers, especially for liquids. Their uses range from fermenting strong alcohols to preserving pickled foods or storing homemade juices, making them the preferred choice for high-capacity storage.
Bedside Chest | 2 sp | 5 lb | loot
    For the commoners who cannot afford a bedside dresser or wardrobe, a Bedside Chest offers a cost-effective alternative for storing all forms of clothing.
Bench | 3 sp | 4 lb | loot
    Wooden benches are frequently used in outdoor terraces and busy taverns due to their ability to seat multiple people. Usually purchased in bulk as they have a limited lifespan due to constant use.
Bucket | 5 cp | 2 lb | loot
    While buckets are commonly used to transport water from wells to homes or for cleaning tasks, they can also be repurposed as makeshift latrines in extreme situations or certain jail cells where outhouses are not an option.
Cannon | 100 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Unlike the ballista, cannons are designed for offensive use, largely because they are mounted on wheels. These wheels serve multiple purposes, they make the cannon easier to transport by sea or road and absorb the force of its powerful recoil. Earlier models of cannons without wheels often had a habit of shattering due to the recoil.
Carved Wooden Toy | 1 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Carving is one of the favorite activities for traveling merchants or adventurers on long voyages to pass the time. It is easy to learn and practice on the go, and it only requires a small carving knife. The toys or trinkets made from it are either sold to stores or given to close family members. Alternative icons exist in the module's folder
Chair | 15 cp | 2 lb | loot
    Wooden chairs, often found in houses, stores, inns, or taverns, are usually made from planks to allow for easy replacement of parts. This is particularly beneficial in busy venues where chairs may need to be replaced following events like brawls.
Coffin | 3 sp | 10 lb | loot
    A coffin is a funerary container used to display or store a corpse before burial or cremation. Depending on cultural traditions and beliefs, it might be reinforced with heavy chains or made of silver, often at great expense, to protect against night creatures.
Cutting Board | 1 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Cutting boards are made from leftover lumber, which is glued and shaped into practical kitchen tools. As customers quite rarely purchase new boards, carpenters add a personal touch by offering custom logos or names burned into the surface, making each piece unique.
Fan | 25 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Primarily made out of paper, or rarely from cloth, and reinforced with either light metals, such as tin, or light woods, like redwood. The fans feature intricate designs, with many crafted to reflect regional symbols or family crests. Some fans, adorned with vibrant colors or delicate embroidery, are considered status symbols and are cherished as heirlooms. On a blistering summer day, villagers unfold their fans with a flick of the wrist, a synchronized dance of fabric and wood, creating a gentle breeze that brings momentary relief from the sun's relentless heat.
Foraging Backpack | 1 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Crafted from straw and reinforced with light wood like willow, this sturdy basket is perfect for gatherers and herbalists. It’s designed for foraging in woodland areas, by the rivers, or across open fields, providing a lightweight yet durable tool for collecting food and herbs.
Fruit Press | 10 gp | 120 lb | loot
    Heavy oak frame with a wooden screw that is turned down onto a slatted tub that can crush many fruits, squeezing the juice out into a trough below. Most villages share a single press that is moved from orchard to orchard in autumn, and its owner takes a portion of every barrel as payment. The leftover pulp is fed to the animals, which tend to wander about tipsy.
Gallows | 5 gp | 1 lb | loot
    One single upright post with a beam at the top forms the basic structure of the gallows, designed for executing death row inmates guilty of horrendous acts. Beyond punishment, they have served as public displays of justice or, in some cases, as a warning to the people after the unrest, often at the direction of the regional higher class.
Grinder | 1 gp | 1 lb | loot
    A wooden contraption made with finely crafted and durable iron cogwheels is designed to reduce materials into a fine powder. A crank allows for easy operation. Though it originally served as an alternative to the traditional mortar and pestle, it is now primarily used by taverns to quickly grind dried pepper.
Hand Cart | 1 gp | 1 lb | loot
    The all-round tool for transporting items where they are needed. It can be used to transport feed for animals, large objects like planks, or carry fruits from orchards, all while remaining lightweight, making it a must-have tool on most farms.
Hand Truck | 1 gp | 10 lb | loot
    A hand truck, also referred to as a hand cart, comes with long handles is perfect for moving heavy items like barrels and furniture. It is frequently used by inns, taverns, and bakeries to facilitate daily restocking from the market.
Jewelry Box | 2 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Jewelry boxes are essential in protecting delicate pieces from damage. While diamonds, being the hardest natural material, can scratch softer gemstones like opals and pearls, they can also mark softer metals like gold. But in general, the regular jewelry boxes provide a dedicated space for jewelry in a centralized storage, reducing the chances of misplacement or loss.
Ladder | 2 sp | 1 lb | loot
    The ladder is a simple yet essential tool for reaching elevated areas. Whether in libraries, homes, farms, barns, or used by firefighters to rescue people from high places, ladders are used in various environments for essential tasks.
Pallet | 4 sp | 10 lb | loot
    A standard bed made by carpenters for a good night's sleep usually consists of straw or hay covered with a sheet. This type of mattress is known as a palliasse or pallet, which is how it got its name.
Pillory | 4 sp | 10 lb | loot
    The pillory is a device used for publicly punishing repeat offenders. It features a wooden frame with holes to secure the head and hands.
Sailing Mast | 25 gp | 150 lb | loot
    The sailing mast is a pole that rises vertically from a ship and supports the sails. Larger sailboats often have more than one mast. The mast plays a crucial role in supporting the sails, allowing the wind to propel the ship.
Sextant | 25 sp | 1 lb | loot
    The sextant is a navigation instrument designed to measure the angular distance between two visible objects. Primarily, it is used to measure the angle between an astronomical body, such as a star or a moon, and the horizon, which is crucial for nautical navigation.
Sled | 25 cp | 3 lb | loot
    Sleds are designed in various shapes and sizes to accommodate various scenarios. They range from children's sleds, perfect for racing down large hills, to sturdy traveling sleds used by sled dogs to traverse vast winter landscapes.
Spinning Wheel | 125 cp | 20 lb | loot
    A spinning wheel, or spindle, is a mechanical device used for spinning thread or yarn from fibers. It converts raw materials such as wool or cotton into usable thread through a process of twisting and winding, which is integral to fabric creation.
Stool | 1 sp | 1 lb | loot
    A standard wooden stool used in many taverns is often repurposed as an improvised weapon in the event of a brawl.
Table | 5 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Standard tables are used to furnish both indoor and outdoor spaces of inns and taverns. They are usually made from planks for easy replacement and maintenance, providing surfaces for patrons to enjoy drinks and food.
Toy Horse | 15 cp | 2 lb | loot
    The toy horse provides a sense of adventure for young children, crafted primarily from wood and often accompanied by wooden toy swords and shields. It sparks the imagination and is a must for the new aspiring adventurers.
Training Dummy | 25 sp | 20 lb | loot
    While quite rare and often quickly replaced by living partners, training dummies serve the purpose of training new combatants in body positions and strike techniques. Due to their fragility and tendency to break with minimal use, they are rare.
Transport Cage | 1 sp | 2 lb | loot
    This wooden cage is designed for the easy transportation of small livestock, such as hens, chickens, and small hares, facilitating their movement to and from local markets with ease.
Vegetable Box | 1 sp | 1 lb | loot
    The box is designed for storing and transporting vegetables and fruits. Its stackable design makes it quite convenient for easy transport.
Wardrobe | 1 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Due to the considerable amount of materials involved, wardrobes are generally made to order by carpenters in smaller towns or villages.
Wooden Ladle | 5 cp | 0.2 lb | loot
    The ladle plays a crucial role in most kitchens. Wooden ladles are specifically used with expensive or cast-iron pots to prevent scratching and extend their lifespan.
Wooden Mannequin | 15 sp | 5 lb | loot
    Wooden mannequins, also known as laymen or lay figures, have many uses, ranging from artists to leatherworkers. Artists utilize these mannequins as molds for their sculptures, applying a layer of gypsum that is later removed to reveal the final form. Meanwhile, leatherworkers shape leather around these figures to craft hide armor and other equipment tailored to fit a humanoid form.
Wooden Ornamentation | 5 gp | 12 lb | loot
    In some regions, common folk enjoy extensive ornamentation on their houses, ships, and even fences. Though it tends to be an expensive project, the end result is visually appealing for generations to come.Additional icons can be found in the module folder
Wooden Pipes | 2 gp | 0.2 lb | loot
    Wooden pipes, or tobacco pipes, are specifically designed for smoking tobacco and herbs. They are typically constructed from strong, hardwoods like briar or corn cob, each offering benefits such as durability and a unique smoking flavor.
Wooden Wheel | 2 sp | 1 lb | loot
    The wheel is a vital invention with widespread applications in society. It is used as a main component in carts, wagons, and carriages, and it is also been used in executions, such as with the breaking wheel. Its importance is evident in various aspects of a commoner's life.

== Tools & Craft / General Tools (58) ==

Anvil | 50 gp | 100 lb | loot
