    Forged from a single block of iron, rarely from ingots, and layered with hardened steel by skilled smiths, with an extended horn for bending metal into shape. A good anvil costs more than a young smith earns in a year, so most are bought secondhand or inherited, and they outlast several generations of smiths. When travelers arrive in a village during the day, they very quickly hear the sound of one being hammered, knowing that there's a smith nearby.
Bolt | 2 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Bolts, nuts, and screws are indispensable in various aspects of life, serving roles in the construction of tools, locks, clocks, furniture, and even in fastening together robust pieces of metal equipment. Noble individuals, particularly those clad in heavy-wearing armor, often retain the services of a dedicated armorer who accompanies them at all times. This skilled armorer carries essential tools of the trade, including a hammer, replacement bolts, wire cutter, and nail puller, ensuring that the noble's armor remains in optimal condition wherever their journey takes them.
Breathing Aparatus | 5 gp | 7 lb | loot
    This mechanized device created for the sole purpose of assisting with relaxing hobbies, such as diving, but also useful in the deepest mines where breathing is a luxury. Primarily made out of copper to avoid rust, the device is quite heavy due to its material, and only trained professionals are advised to use it.
Byrne | 15 sp | 2 lb | loot
    These belts were typically made from leather, woven braid, embroidered fabric or solid metalwork. Commoner women favored sturdier and more practical versions made from leather or tightly woven wool, as they mostly used them as a stand-in for a corset. Most belts remained plain apart from the buckle, while men often chose theirs to display status and personal pride.
Carpenters Plane | 8 sp | 2 lb | loot
    A plane, featuring a cleverly hafted cutting edge, is meticulously designed to skin or shave the surface of wood. Employed for finishing and leveling surfaces, it adeptly erases marks left by earlier tools such as an ax, hammer, or saw, leaving the wood smooth, flat, and straight
Chess Piece | 10 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Carving chess pieces is a complex task, requiring precise craftsmanship to maintain a consistent style across the set. Each variant of chess, like Courier or Tamerlane, calls for different pieces, adding another layer of complexity and skill to the creation process.
Chisel | 3 sp | 1 lb | loot
    This cutting tool is employed by various trades dealing with heavier materials, serving in dressing, shaping, or working solid materials such as wood, stone, or metal. Typically, it is struck with a mallet to ensure precision.
Detonator | 5 sp | 2 lb | loot
    A tool highly favored by miners, its combination with gunpowder explosive facilitates the rapid creation of holes or tunnels to reach the more interesting metals beneath the ground. Nevertheless, due to the occasional instability of the explosions and the rare risk of shrapnel damaging mine supports, drills tend to be the preferred choice.
Distilation Gear | 1 gp | 2 lb | loot
    Many alchemists and taverns keep one because it is used to improve the quality of potions and drinks. Creating the finest potions or strongest beverages require its use, but since the process takes a great deal of time, the final product often becomes very expensive.
Drill | 15 sp | 5 lb | loot
    This handheld mechanical drill operates through gears and oil, spinning in a steady circular motion to dig deeper into a single point. It requires skilled miners familiar with tinkering, as the rocky terrain often wears down the drill head, demanding frequent replacement.
Exploding Stick | 3 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Exploding sticks are usually named that way for drilling purposes, to create small cracks in heavy solid rock or ore so that a pickaxe or a drill can follow up. There is usually a specialized machinist who makes them using the finest gunpowder and also sets them up. They will not produce a big bang, but they will get the job done in clearing rocks.
Fat Candles | 1 cp | 2 lb | loot
    Fat candles are crafted from rendered animal fat, such as whale blubber or tallow from sheep and cows. Although beeswax candles last longer, these candles burn more brightly and cost significantly less. The downside is that they release an unpleasant foul odor while burning.
Flat Tongs | 5 sp | 0 lb | loot
    These particular tongs are available in various shapes, ranging from a slender design crafted for creating small indentations in metals to broader variants designed for handling firey-hot metals. They stand out as some of the most versatile types of tongs, serving the purpose of picking up and gripping hot pieces of flat metal, preferred by most novice blacksmiths.
Garden Hoe | 1 sp | 1 lb | loot
    The garden hoe serves multiple purposes, assisting with seeding by creating small trenches for seed placement and aiding in weed removal. With its sharp and wide blade, farmers favor the garden hoe for cutting rows of weeds. It effortlessly slides just beneath the soil's surface, cutting the roots of pesky weeds.
Gear | 4 cp | 1 lb | loot
    An regular gear piece that fits into everything mechanized, from clocks to doors to pressure valves, it is a specialized tool for all mechanized items and structures. Without it, they would not exist. Primarily made out of copper, brass, or bronze, it resists rust and lasts for decades.
Glider Wings | 4 cp | 3 lb | loot
    While few understand the intricate workings of aerodynamics, some who do use a pair of semi-mechanized wings to travel faster than on foot. They are usually made from a very light aluminum frame with strengthened cloth, similar to that used on the largest ship sails.
Gloves Of Working | 15 sp | 2 lb | loot
    Gloves were worn by outdoor workers for practical purposes, such as gardening or heavy outdoor work. These working gloves were often made of leather and lined with fur, or alternatively, made of sheepskin with the fleece on the inside.
Grandfather Clock | 12 cp | 15 lb | loot
    Grandfather clock, also known as a longcase clock, tall-case clock, hall clock, or floor clock, is a tall, freestanding, weight-driven pendulum clock with the pendulum held inside the tower or waist of the case. These are created by the most experienced woodworkers, with help from specialized engineers.
Hand Axe | 5 gp | 2 lb | loot
    The go-to axe for various applications, it proves its usefulness as a camping tool for felling small trees, splitting kindling, and preparing firewood. Craftsmen appreciate its finesse in cutting wood to size and creating intricate patterns on furniture.
Hand Drill | 1 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Functioning as a manual tool, a hand drill transforms and magnifies the circular motion of the crank into the rotation of a drill chuck. The tool includes a cranking handle that turns pinion gears on the main shaft, with a chuck at the end designed to secure a drill bit. This versatile tool finds common use among masons, carpenters, leatherworkers, and even blacksmiths, facilitating the drilling of fine holes in hard materials such as wood, hard leather, stone, soft metals, or even bones.
Hand Saw | 2 sp | 1.5 lb | loot
    With a wide range of applications, hand saws are the go-to tools for tasks like roughly cutting wood and logs, as well as making intricate curved cuts in wood. Whether used in professional trades or by the commoner for projects at home or in the garden, they prove to be indispensable tools.
Horseshoe | 1 sp | 1 lb | loot
    In certain regions, horseshoes are seen as a harbinger of good luck, while in others, they symbolize advanced industry. Typically crafted from steel, the choice of material may vary depending on metal availability in specific areas, including options like bronze, copper, or iron. In exceptionally uncommon cases, materials such as hide, leather, or even wood are considered.
Hot Punch | 3 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Referred to either as a forging punch or hot punch, this tool is primarily utilized for cutting or punching through metal to shape and secure it. Moreover, it lends itself to forming impressions or designs, making it a choice for the more discerning customers.
Hourglass | 25 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Two glass bulbs joined at a narrow waist and held in a turned wooden frame, filled with fine dry sand that takes a set time to run from top to bottom. Ships use them to time the watches, courts to limit speeches, and preachers to measure sermons, though preachers are known to turn theirs over more than once.
Jackhammer | 25 sp | 3 lb | loot
    Jackhammer is a pneumatic, steam-powered, or arcano-mechanical tool that combines a hammer directly with a chisel. It was created specifically to chisel out harder stones that do not require finesse to break into smaller pieces for easier transportation.
Mallet | 3 sp | 3 lb | loot
    Wooden mallets are a staple in carpentry, serving to knock wooden pieces together, drive dowels, manipulate chisels, and apply pressure on joints. Notably, they stand apart from most metal hammers by preventing the deformation of the striking end of a metal tool.
Mason Hammer | 3 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Commonly called brick hammers, mason's hammers are primarily designed for the precise breaking of bricks, rocks, pavers, or driving stakes. They feature one flat traditional face and, on the back, a short or long chisel-shaped blade. This design allows them to effectively chip off edges or small pieces of stone and cut brick masonry units without the need for a separate chisel.
Mechanized Key | 1 gp | 1 lb | loot
    These types of keys are more like missing pieces in a complex engineered structure. They are called keys because when they are placed into their designated slots, they activate the mechanism of the structure. These keys are typically used for vaults, and only a very few are capable of crafting them.
Mine Cart | 1 gp | 200 lb | loot
    Created for the sole purpose of removing debris from the mines, they can also be used to transport miners deeper to their working spots. They are usually carried back to the entrance at the end of the day by either commoners or livestock, but in some areas rope-haulage systems have been developed to make pulling easier.
Mine Rails | 25 gp | 200 lb | loot
    Made out of steel to sustain the heavy loads from carts carrying ore and rocks. Blacksmiths, when crafting these, are required to use the most pristine materials they have in stock to ensure they stand the test of time and do not bend under extreme pressures and temperatures.
Nails | 5 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Nails are commonly made out of bronze or iron, and are mainly used as fasteners in construction, from small roofing nails to large iron spikes. They are also occasionally made out of copper or steel, particularly in environments where corrosion resistance is a key factor, such as coastal or riverside areas.
Nippers Tongs | 4 sp | 3 lb | loot
    Crafted mainly from steel, also referred to as farrier nippers, mirror the appearance of a giant pair of nail clippers. Their primary function involves removing a previously placed horseshoe. Beyond that, these tools prove adept at cutting the flat end of bolts or nails, resulting in a smoother finish for armor, furniture, or even walls.
Oil Lamp | 1 gp | 1 lb | loot
    They work in the same way as a candle but with fuel that is liquid at room temperature, so that a container for the oil is required. A textile wick drops down into the oil, and is lit at the end, burning the oil as it is drawn up the wick. Sources of fuel for oil lamps include a wide variety of plants such as nuts and seeds but also widely used are animal fats.
Padlock | 10 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Heavy iron lock with a curved shackle and a keyhole on its face or side, made by smiths who guard their designs as closely as the goods their locks protect. Merchants use them on chests, gates, and warehouse doors, and each is sold with a matching key and a spare.
Paring Chisel | 2 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Paring chisels, favored by most carpenters, are designed for delicately shaving thin slivers of wood. Their primary use is in carefully shaving off thin amounts of wood when fitting joints and the long length of these chisels provides carpenters with maximum control.
Pickaxe | 2 gp | 3 lb | loot
    Pickaxes, also referred to as mattocks, are tools used for breaking up rocks, paving, or earth, commonly employed in activities such as digging, soil preparation, demolition, or specialized applications like mining.
Pitchfork | 1 sp | 2 lb | loot
    The pitchfork, commonly referred to as a hay fork, is a tool crafted for pitching loose materials, including hay, straw, manure, or leaves. With its long handle and typically two to five thin tines, it is specifically designed for the efficient movement of such materials.
Pocketwatch | 1 gp | 1 lb | loot
    The pocket watches come in two different types. A hunter-case pocket watch is a case with a spring-hinged circular metal lid or cover, that closes over the watch-dial, protecting them from dust, scratches and other damage or debris while an open-faced is one in which the case lacks a metal cover to protect the front glass.
Pouch | 5 sp | 2 lb | loot
    The simple is mainly made from simple cloth, sturdy leather, or fine silk and features a drawstring that threads through reinforced holes in a looping pattern. It is mainly intended to carry coins, though it can also hold materials or small objects depending on the need.
Pressure Gauge | 1 gp | 1 lb | loot
    An pressure gauge is a measuring instrument used to monitor the force within contained systems, such as steam conduits or alchemical apparatus. Some variants are focused on arcane mechanisms, using enchanted fluids or elemental spirits to detect fluctuations in force that would be invisible to the naked eye.
Pressurized Container | 1 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Created specifically to contain the most volatile materials and keep them under pressure, such as specific gases, air, breaths of mythical beasts, or arcane compounds. Usually, if one of these fissures occurs, even though made from sturdy materials, disaster strikes.
Puzzle Box | 1 gp | 1 lb | loot
    This small toy comes with a disclaimer stating that it contains small parts. While it is mechanized, the Puzzle Box is mostly aimed at young children to help them with cognitive development. It contains many small cogs and gears. However, when it is finally opened, parents always leave a treat for the children.
Rake | 1 gp | 2 lb | loot
    Rakes come in various types, but the two most common are garden rakes and soil rakes. Garden rakes are effectively designed to pick up leaves and other garden debris without causing damage to the lawn and the soil beneath. On the other hand, soil rakes are intended for leveling and grading the soil before plants and seeds are planted.
Scarecrow | 2 sp | 0 lb | loot
    The scarecrow, rooted in popular tradition, is a mannequin typically stuffed with straw. Its primary purpose is to deter birds or other animals from consuming seeds, crops, and fruit.
Scythe | 1 gp | 2 lb | loot
    The scythe is a tool predominantly utilized by farmers for mowing grass, grain, or other crops, the scythe consists of a long, curving blade attached at an angle to an extended handle. In certain regions, it carries an association with death, a belief particularly held by the most superstitious commoners.
Sewing Needle | 2 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    A sewing needle, utilized for hand-sewing and crafted from steel or bone, is a long slender tool with a pointed tip at one end and a hole, or eye, designed to hold the sewing thread.
Shovel | 2 gp | 2 lb | loot
    The Shovel is a tool used to dig as well as to move loose dirt, gravel, grain, or snow, from one spot to another. It can also be used for making trenches and uprooting deep-rooted weeds and plants.
Sickle | 2 sp | 1 lb | loot
    The sickle being one of the most common hand tools, finds extensive use in harvesting crops, cutting grass, and assisting in the gathering of vegetables from fields. The versatility of its blade extends to having a major role as a weapon in certain regions.
Spade | 7 sp | 2 lb | loot
    The spade is perfect for planting and transplanting, spades feature a flat head design that is particularly effective for digging holes or extracting plants from the ground.
Spectacles | 75 gp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Ground glass lenses set in thin gold or brass rims, held on the nose by a pinch and tied to a cord so they are not lost. Scholars, scribes, jewelers, and aging merchants all depend on them, and a good pair can cost as much, if not more, as a horse. Lens grinders and clockmakers are few that can make lenses, so most buyers simply try on pairs at the market stall until they find one that helps.
Spindle | 5 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Commonly made from either wood, bone or metal, the spindle is a rod for spinning wool, silk, and other natural fibers. Among weavers and tailors, it stands out as one of their most cherished tools.
Stopwatch | 1 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Mechanical stopwatches are powered by a mainspring, which must be wound up, usually by turning the knurled knob at the top of the stopwatch. They are primarily used to measure elapsed time in various fields such as sports, training, and science for activities like running laps or conducting experiments.
Storm Lantern | 5 gp | 2 lb | loot
    Tin lantern with a glass chimney and a wire guard, burning oil through an adjustable wick, and made to stay lit in wind and rain. Sailors, watchmen, miners, and farmers checking on their animals at night all rely on them. It holds enough oil for a full night, though the glass blackens with soot and needs wiping every few days.
Thimble | 5 cp | 0.02 lb | loot
    Resembling a small cap, typically made of metal or bone, the thimble is worn over the fingertip to safeguard it while pushing a needle through cloth during sewing. A cherished tool among tailors and leatherworkers, as it is indispensable in their toolset.
Timed Explosive | 1 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Timed explosives are devices that detonate after a predetermined delay, often using a timer to trigger the explosion, giving just enough time to get out of harm's way. The delayed explosions allow fragmented rock to move before the next charge detonates, maximizing the blast's effectiveness and reducing vibrations that could damage the structure of the mines.
Timer | 1 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Usually associated with explosives, for situations where multiple explosions need to happen simultaneously, an amazing piece of engineering, whether for the demolition of a gate wall or a sturdy mine wall. The timer is a necessity for a planned chained reaction. However, it is also used in sporting events to provide a clear indication of a winner.
Transport Shackles | 25 sp | 1 lb | loot
    These shackles, made from iron or steel, like their counterparts and intended for easy mass production, function similar to pillories. They are typically designed to link several sets of shackles in a daisy chain to facilitate the transport of prisoners between locations.
Wolf Jaw Tongs | 5 sp | 0 lb | loot
    Considered the most universal tongs for general work of varying sizes, wolf jaw tongs feature smooth, narrow jaws on top with a V-notch on the inside running its length. This makes them one of the most essential tools in the tool belt of any blacksmith.

== Tools & Craft / Herbalist Supplies (115) ==

Adder Venom | 50 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Adders even if it is a poisonous snake, it is rarely fatal for the regular commoner, however, alchemists do keep them in glass cages to harvest their venom for potions and poisons. Long ago, scales of the snakes were used to create some beautiful egg-shaped bombs - which as we have learned from scrolls - were quite destructive.
Amanita Caesareae | 5 cp | 0.3 lb | loot
    This mushroom is easily known by its distinctive orange cap above yellow gills. Though it closely resembles poisonous species such as the fly agaric, it is in fact edible and desired by cooks at the table. Even so, the resemblance and mistake is deadly, and herbalists advise that any different coloured mushroom be eaten only after their review.
Amaranth | 3 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Commonly referred to as redroot pigweed, it is primarily used for grain, and then further processed to flour. Due to its quick explosive growth, the three sisters agricultural method is preferred with them, providing a mixture of yields at the moment of harvest.
Arcana Magma | 200 gp | 0.5 lb | loot
    Lava looking dust gathered from the arcane monsters that roam the realms. Adventurers will try their luck selling whatever they can muster from such a creature, and the bolder among them experiment with the dust themselves, hoping to create something new and valuable. Does happen quite often, to the detriment of the experienced alchemists' warnings, that not every experiment ends well.
Bearberry | 15 gp | 1 lb | loot
    A lot of gatherers, herbalists, and alchemists grow Bearberries around their workplaces, as it is assumed that they attract bees and hummingbirds, to help with pollination and honey, and butterflies to harvest their wings. The Bearberry fruit is used very loosely in medicine, mainly in medicinal teas. Bearberries are also used by Leatherworkers as a tanning agent to turn hides into leather.
Belladonna Fruit | 125 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Belladonna Fruit is one of the most toxic fruits known throughout the realms. As such, it is only farmed and harvested by the most skilled Gatherers. Without the proper expertise and knowledge, inexperienced folks might be fooled into eating the cherry-like fruit. After being processed, it is sometimes used in cosmetics as its effects are considered to have an attractive and seductive quality. When a solution is dropped in the eyes it dilates the pupils. The plant has multiple applications, however, its main usage is to create very potent poisons. It is speculated even Emperors have fallen to it.
Bitterroot | 1 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Bitterroot is a plant that is prized for its beautiful flowers, which come in a range of colors and can survive in most climates. However, it is not the flowers that are in demand, but the roots of the plant. Bitterroot roots are considered a delicacy and are widely sought after. Some people believe that consuming bitterroot before a journey will help to keep wild beasts at bay.
Blewits | 4 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Blewits are strong and delicious mushrooms, quickly identified by their faint purple coloring. Look for them in the fall in mulch piles, and sauté them for dinner. Quite often they are mistaken for a different mushroom called "Amethyst Deceiver", however, both are edible.
Blue Chanterelle | 2 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Blue Chanterelle fruiting bodies can be described as having funnel shaped caps with a velvety texture that often grow stacked upon one another. The wild Blue Chanterelle mushroom is believed to be rare due to its association with old growth coniferous forests. However, this belief may be a fabrication, as the species has also been spotted, though rarely, in grassland environments.
Blue Meanies | 3 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Blue Meanies can be found on compost across the realms. They are relatively small, easily known and spotted by their iridescent blue caps that catch the light. For all their delicate appearance, herbalists warn against consuming them under any circumstances, for the mushrooms are quite toxic.
Bone Meal | 1 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Made from finely ground bone, this pale meal is used as a fertiliser. Rather than enriching the soil all at once, it releases its nutrients slowly over the course of a few months. Butchers and tanners gladly sell their bone scraps to the millers who prepare it.
Breadseed Poppy | 12 sp | 0.1 lb | loot
    The breadseed poppy is a decorative plant often found in gardens. It is grown for its seeds, oil, and medicinal properties. When combined with strong alcohol, it can be used as a powerful pain reliever.
Brute's Mace | 5 cp | 0.3 lb | loot
    Quite often mistaken for the Spiky Bloom, which it closely resembles, though the two could not be more different. Where its sister mushroom is deadly, the Brute's Mace is harmless if handled correctly, peel away the hardened layer of spikes and the tender flesh beneath has an amazing taste.
Butterfly Fruit | 4 cp | 0.2 lb | loot
    Hook shaped, this fruit is dried and used as ornamentation for furniture, with some additional carving, its curve used as a hook for hanging clothes. In the more impoverished villages it occasionally finds its way into the cooking pot instead, used as a base for soups as its flavour is quite sour.
Carnation | 4 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Commonly known as Carnation or clove pink, and besides the ornamental aspect of them, where most houses have a potted flower. The Carnations, after turning in a paste with honey, are used by some herbalists to treat issues such as skin problems, hair loss, and muscle tension.
Chamomile | 4 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Most common folk, when out and about, tend to keep an eye out for Chamomiles, as it grows in the wild. Its use is mainly as a sedative for patients when made into medicinal tea.
Clover | 1 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Clover has long been used as a medicinal herb, by most herbalists, reputed to help everything from cramps to asthma. However, the clover's primary use is mainly used as feed for livestock or added as a fertilizer. In some regions, it is believed that if you find a four-leaf clover, Lady Luck will smile at you. However, most mistake the four-leaf clover with the Wood Sorrel.
Clustered Domecap | 2 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Known as the Clustered Domecap or the Fried Chicken Mushroom, it earns its alternative name because its unique taste, even when raw, resembles the flavor of fried poultry. This specific mushroom is in very high demand by inns and taverns, as they can incorporate it into many recipes that taste, quite frankly, amazing.
Coneflower | 8 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Primarily used in teas and tinctures, the coneflower is found in the gardens of commoners, who keep it close as it is a really good home remedy, but besides its patching up benefits it is simply a pretty flower to look at in the garden.
Cranesbill | 1 cp | 3 lb | loot
    Cranesbill is a garden plant, that has multiple medicinal uses. Most commoners keep them around their houses to make tea. Cranesbill is an herb that can be used as a mouth rinse to soothe the pain of canker sores, and also made as a hot brew, to soothe stringent stomach aches.
Daer-Kron | 5 gp | 1 lb | loot
    To plant the Dark-Kron seed, a miner picks a small round hole, places the seed inside, and uses either ground bones mixed with wax as a nutrient source, or a combination of wood, charcoal, and glue to plug the hole. After three days, a soft light begins to emanate from the wall where the Daer-Kron seed was planted. Slowly, a mushroom unfurls, casting an eerie but comforting glow throughout the chamber, called Neon-Ront. For those navigating the underground depths, these fungi are not just lights but beacons, guiding them safely through dark tunnels.
Dandelion | 1 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Dandelion leaves add flavor to salads, sandwiches, and teas. The roots are used in some coffee substitutes, and the flowers are used to make wines. Dandelions are definitely safe to eat both raw and cooked, and you can eat almost all parts of the dandelion. They can also be used for medicinal purposes, as they are one of the best greens that can be used in anti-inflammation tinctures.
Dead Heart Apple | 5 cp | 0.3 lb | loot
    Despite its name, this fruit shares none of the properties of an actual apple, least of all being edible. It earned the name as, from a distance, its shape and colour resemble one. Already dry upon the branch and foul to the taste, it has little appeal to be eaten. Its primary use lies with the herbalists, who grind it into foul smelling poultices.
Death Cap | 125 cp | 1 lb | loot
    The most favored mushroom by the most unfavorable people. The consumption of the Death Cap is more often than not fatal. In most forests close to civilization, signs are put up to warn people against eating random mushrooms due to the Death Caps growing almost anywhere.
Desert Shaggy Mane | 2 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Large numbers of these mushrooms may appear after soaking rains. They thrive in deserts and in very harsh climates. A weary adventurer might eat them in a pinch, as when the mushroom is young, it is edible. However, herbalists find these mushrooms quite difficult to cultivate due to the specifics of their natural habitats.
Divine Light | 1 gp | 0.2 lb | loot
    This flower, once it reaches full bloom, gives off both a pleasant smell and a soft, steady glow. During the warm months, common folk gather it and keep it indoors as it emits a gentle light, sparing themselves the cost of candles or lantern oil.
Elfin Saddle | 3 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Known as the Fluted Black Elfin Saddle, Black Helvella, or Slate Grey Saddle, this is a slightly toxic yet very unusual mushroom. Some herbalists believe it is edible, however, not all agree, as some have identified issues, including pained stomachs, among commoners who consumed it.
Field Mushroom | 2 cp | 1 lb | loot
    The Field Mushroom, also known as the Meadow Mushroom, is one of the most frequently misidentified mushrooms. It is commonly confused with the toxic Yellow Stainer, which bears a close resemblance, and also with the deadly Death Cap. Due to these risks, herbalists advise that foragers without proper expertise avoid picking random mushrooms in meadows and on the fields.
Figworts | 6 cp | 0 lb | loot
    Figworts are a coveted herb, as a lot of herbalists look for them as they can be of use in curing skin inflammation, pain reliever, or even as a mild laxative. Most Figworts are usually found in rich woodlands, and when the gatherers go into the forest, they attempt to keep an eye out for them, as herbalists tend to pay a premium for them.
Fly Agaric | 4 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Fly Agaric is sometimes called in dire times "The Graveyard Keeper" due to its toxic proprieties. However, even with warning some do still consume it as it is believed that it makes a connection with the deities with often fatal results. The mushroom's primary use is as an insecticide, when it is combined with milk, it both attracts and kills flies, which also gave it its name.
Forest Lantern | 5 sp | 0.5 lb | loot
    Usually gathered by alchemists, then dried and bundled together for storage. Once the bundle is lit, it throws off a powerful but short-lived light that burns through it quickly. For this reason it is saved for emergencies and used mostly at night as it behaves very similar to a flare, high burst of light, for a very short period.
Funeral Bell | 3 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Funeral Bell, Deadly Skullcap, Autumn Skullcap, or Deadly Galerina, this mushroom is, as the name suggests, quite deadly. It is unfortunately often confused with several other edible mushrooms. A grim belief holds that after consuming one, the victim will hear a church bell forecasting their demise, no matter where they are.
Garlic Scapes | 2 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Garlic Scapes are an herb with a stronger garlicky flavor than common chives. They are primarily used for pickling or seeding and are rarely used in cooking or as a stir-fry base for scrambled eggs. In some regions, garlic scapes are considered a seasonal delicacy, eagerly awaited each spring. Local markets fill with bundles of these curly green stalks, signaling the start of warmer months and inspiring cooks to add a unique twist to traditional recipes.
Gauze | 2 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Primarily made out from linen, its main purpose is cleaning injureies. To help in this, some antiseptics like honey or vinegar are used with these cloths for bandaging, and sometimes medicinal herbs are incorporated to speed up the healing process. Most travelers have a few premade with them at all times.
God's Wrath | 5 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Sometimes called Brimstone, and more commonly known as Sulfur. Alchemists usually use blocks of sulfur in creating potent acids that can burn through anything. It is often at the center of disputes between alchemical and religious groups, as by some religions it is believed to be sacred, given how it burns in a unique color with the flames a rich purple shade. Alchemists however advise against burning the blocks as they believe that doing so can inflict irreparable damage to one's health. Herbalists use it to create multiple types of green dyes.
Gold-Drop Milkcap | 5 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Known as the Gold-Drop or Yellowdrop Milkcap, this mushroom is usually found on or near oak trees. Worse still, it can be found intermingled with other mushrooms in a symbiotic system. However, unlike the mushrooms is usually close by, this milkcap is rather lethal. Herbalists advise that during the harvest season, each mushroom should be triple checked and verified by someone familiar with the regional mushrooms.
Golden Berry | 3 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    The cape gooseberry is sometimes referred to as either ground cherry or golden berry, due to its yellow color. Most of the time, it is eaten as is or dipped in sweet chocolate. It can also be cooked to make exquisite jams, jellies, and purées, or used in exotic fruit salads, pavlovas, and roulades. Additionally, it can be simmered in water with a little sugar and used in fruit pies, cakes, or crumbles.
Gooseberries | 3 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    The gooseberry plant, while adored by wildlife, plays a critical role as both a food source and a vector for disease. Its berries provide essential nutrients for many animals, including birds and bears, who rely on the fruit for sustenance during the summer months. However, its rapid spread also means that it is often seen as an invasive threat to local flora, particularly coniferous trees like pines, which can suffer long-term damage from the blister rust fungus it harbors.
Green Elf Cup | 3 gp | 0.2 lb | loot
    Can be found on oak branches or trees, stained a beautiful turquoise, if you see the wood that color the Green Elf Cup has taken hold. The mushroom itself has no practical use, but the wood it is attached to is another story entirely. Stained through with that same distinct colour, such timber is wanted by woodworkers and makes it among the most expensive lumber you can find on the market.
Green-Spored Parasol | 2 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Green-Spored Parasol usually harvested by the young commoners for... abstract jokes on others. This mushrooms has another name, simply called as "The Vomiter". As the name implies, it is toxic, but not deadly, but just enough to be an, somewhat large, incovenience for those who consume it. Herbalists advise consuming large amounts of dairy, as that tends to soothe the stomach.
Henbane | 1 sp | 0.1 lb | loot
    The Henbane, also known as the Witches Plant, is a highly toxic plant, similar to the nightshade, however, herbalists use it, in combination with a plethora of other plants, to create anesthetic for sale for any medical practitioners. The Henbane is also a ritualistic herb, being used by magicians and healers in ritual purifications by smoke as well as in the preparation of concoctions, love philters, and 'flying ointments'.
Horsetail | 7 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Horsetail is a common herb that has been used by herbalists for centuries to treat a variety of ailments, including bleeding, wounds, colds, and hay fever. It grows in many parts of the world and is considered poisonous to sheep and cattle, although it is safe for the common folk to consume in small amounts.
Hyacinth | 6 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Hyacinth is a popular ornamental plant known for its fragrant blooms in the spring. While it was originally used to create perfumes, it was found to cause skin irritation and is poisonous when ingested. As a result, it is not recommended for use in this way.
Indigo Milkcap | 5 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Indigo Milkcap, also known as the Indigo Milky or Blue Milk Mushroom, is a strikingly unique, blue to purple mushroom. It is quite common in many markets and usually fetches a high price, largely due to its exotic colors. Innkeepers frequently order them, as local patrons seem particularly interested in their flavor, that is often described as resembling roasted sweet corn or sugar cookies.
Jack'o'lantern Mushroom | 5 sp | 1 lb | loot
    There's as a small belief that these are somewhat halocinogenic, and is speculated that you will see the undead headless horseman after consumption. The only thing that will affect you with, is going to be a very upset and pained stomach. Most commoners are naming these as False Chanterelle as they look very similar.
King Bolete | 3 cp | 1 lb | loot
    King Bolete, also known as the Penny Bun, is one of the largest mushrooms found in forests. While its rather large size is a quick initial indicator, it can also be easily identified by its unique smell, which is reminiscent of sourdough bread. Some commoners refer to them as "forest bread buns", as both their smell and color resemble them.
Kurnarac | 5 gp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Deep within the cave, the Kurnarac seed is carefully planted. With a precise shot from the slingshot or a modified crossbow, it lands against the ceiling, sticking fast. Years pass, and once a small seed begins to grow, its upside-down tree-like form takes shape as its spiky bark gradually thickens. The cold, damp cave becomes the perfect environment for a steady, almost imperceptible growth. Over the decades, its roots stretch downward, like a stalactite forming, until the Kurnarac matures, its bark now a thick, protective shell. The need for harvesting fades, as the plant naturally preserves its Wall Meat within, offering sustenance that will last indefinitely.
Last Hope Fire | 2 gp | 0.2 lb | loot
    In the evening, these mushrooms begin to give off a warm, lamplight coloured glow, and rumors say that they are meant to lead a lost traveller safely home through the dark. Alchemists who have studied them tell a scarier story, the fungus emits no light at all. Its spores induce small hallucinations that only make it seem to glow, and be easily spotted. Most adventurers who had consumed one, very rarely get to tell the tale of it.
Laurel | 2 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    The Laurel comes from an evergreen shrub or small tree, that is usually kept potted at a herbalist's store. The medicinal properties of the Laurel make it one of the most coveted plants by all medicinal practitioners. It can be used in poultices, teas, as a rub, or direct medicine, for any kind of disease to any form of infection. It is also of status, in some regions laurel crowns are quite common, or a symbol of victory or achievement.
Lobster | 3 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Lobster mushroom is a parasite, acting a bit different to many others, as it grows on and clings to certain species of other mushrooms. It causes the them to change shape and color, and its flavor comes to resemble that of cooked lobster. Older harvests are excellent for dehydrating, making this one of the best mushrooms to process into a powder.
Maidenhair | 2 cp | 1 lb | loot
    This herbal supplement is frequently recommended by herbalists and is mainly used to address blood disorders. It is also renowned for its ability to help keep your memory sharp.
Maple Sap | 25 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Being one of the sweetest variants of sap, it can be used by brewmasters to create ales, or by tavern keeps to sweeten water, as a syrup, or even tea. Alchemists use it to create potions that make your aim true.
Maple Seeds | 1 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Maple seeds, known in some regions as samaras, are edible and surprisingly tasty, whether eaten raw or roasted. The young folk know them by a fonder name, flying seeds, for the way they spin and twirl through the air as they fall from the branches, a sight that displays the harvest season is upon them.
Marjoram | 1 cp | 0.01 lb | loot
    Marjoram is quite similar to oregano but is a bit milder and sweeter, a lot of chefs use it for meats, stews, and sauces. Herbalists use it as a primer for any concoctions that help with runny nose, cough, common cold, other infections, and various digestion problems.
Matsutake | 3 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Matsutake mushrooms are hard to find due to their specific growth requirements, and herbalists have difficulty remaking their proper conditions, as they grow in very specific forests. In the harvest season, the commoners usually race the wildlife to find these mushrroms in good conditions for the market, as fully grown one can fetch a pretty penny.
Mistletoe | 2 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Mistletoe is culturally referred to as the kissing plant, for the way it grows as well as being associated with some winter holidays. Mistletoe represents romance, fertility, and vitality. They grow attached to their host tree or shrub through which they extract water and nutrients from the host plant. Used by herbalists everywhere, for blood conditions, such as arthritis, or for those who have conjugal issues in their homes.
Mugwort | 12 cp | 1 lb | loot
    A common herb that is a favorite by herbalists everywhere. As a plant, it is easy to come-by as it grows in all biomes, and is used to resolve digestive problems, used for sedation, even as a laxative for the upset stomach.
Myrica Gale | 5 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Or more commonly known as Sweet gale. It can live in some of the most challenging conditions and is a very well-known and harvested plant. Its uses can range from local home remedies for stomach aches, and insect repellent, to creating flavorful teas, or even sweet ales.
Myrrh | 25 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Herbalists look for Myrrh resin to use in creating perfume, incense, and medicine. It is believed that if the leaf of the Myrrh is chewed on, it even helps with bad breath. In some cultures, it is also mixed in the process of making wine.
Natron | 25 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Natron is harvested directly as a salt mixture from dry salt lake beds, and it has multiple applications by both Alchemist and Herbalists. Herbalists mix Natron with herbs to treat minor wounds and cuts, while Alchemists use it as a binding agent for the more corrosive or explosive tinctures they create.
Neon-Ront | 5 gp | 1 lb | loot
    The Neon-Ront mushroom is located in the densest woodlands, where the light of the sun doesn't protrude from the dense foliage. The Neon-Ront mushroom can be located due to its blue light glow. This specific fungus is in high demand for many civilizations that live underground, as it can be used for shelving as it is quite sturdy, binds well with rocky terrain and walls, and can be cut into any shape. It is also used for indoor illumination.
Nerium | 5 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Most commonly known as Oleander, it is a small shrub used for gardening ornamentation. When used by highly skilled herbalists, they can make sleeping tinctures and cure some types of skin irritations.
Nettle | 5 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Or Stinging Nettle, it is used mainly by practitioners of medicine to help treat muscle pains of the weary adventurers or the joints of the elderly. Experienced Alchemists use them in potions of healing as one of the base ingredients
Nutmeg | 3 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Nutmeg is the seed or the ground spice derived from the seed, of several tree species. Nutmeg and Mace the red cover of the nutmeg seed, is used to flavor baked goods, meat, fish, and vegetables, and in preserving and pickling or for seasonal beverages such as eggnog, mulled cider, and mulled wine. Herbalists use Nutmeg as it may help relieve bloating, cramps, and upset stomachs due to food intolerance or allergies.
Ocean River Strings | 2 sp | 0.2 lb | loot
    Found along the edges of rivers and the deltas where they meet the sea, this algae is known to most simply as Strings. Soldiers have another name for them, they call them war bandages. When proper supplies run low, scouts are sent to the nearest bodies of water to collect as much as they can carry, for the algae proves useful set as improvised bandages.
Old Man Of The Woods | 5 cp | 1 lb | loot
    This mushroom is easily recognized by its shaggy, soft, dark grey and black spots on a white surface, which turns red and black when bruised. Many herbalists recommend this color changing reaction for identification to avoid mistaking it for something a tad bit more deadly. These mushrooms are edible but are mostly purchased by taverns or inns when young, as they become tasteless with age.
Olive Pomace | 5 cp | 1 lb | loot
    After olives are crushed for oil, the remnants are pressed again to extract any remaining oil and then dried into a fine powder. For ease of transportation, the powder is shaped into small pellets or cylindrical bricks. These bricks are used as fuel in bakeries, as their unique burning smell, combined with the aroma of baked goods, attracts considerable clientele and other establishments. Manors, castles, and forts often order them in large quantities for their braziers due to their long burn time. However, they are highly flammable and can ignite in direct sunlight, necessitating specific storage. They require storage in barrels with salted water to prevent mold and mitigate the fire risk.
Onion Sprout | 3 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    A sprouting onion won't grow more onions in the current year. Instead, it will eventually flower and begin seeding. Afterward, the seeds can grow onions for the following year. By separating the sprouts inside an onion's layers and planting them in good soil, you can produce multiple new onions within a single season.
Oyster | 45 gp | 1 lb | loot
    They are usually harvested for food and for their rare pearls. Wizards on occasion use their shells as magical components, however, the spell it is used for has been kept a secret. In times long past, there was word that rare green emerald oysters had existed as well, however, no one has seen one lately. Some Taverns offer Oysters as a delicacy depending on the region.
Peach Pits | 1 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Peach trees are notoriously finicky when it comes to planting, demanding patience and knowledge. Their seeds need to be kept in cold storage for an extended period before they’re ready for planting. This period of dormancy ensures that when the time comes to plant, the seed will sprout and grow into a healthy tree. Even a small mistake in timing or storage can result in failure, which is why only the most experienced farmhands and herbalists take on the responsibility of planting the pits.
Pennyroyal | 14 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Herbalists are on a high lookout for the Pennyroyal plant, when ground down into oils it is used for the trusty four, or two-legged companions as a flea replant. Its also usually used in soaps and fragrances for perfumes as it smells very similar to spearmint when crushed.
Pink Waxcaps | 1 sp | 0 lb | loot
    Pink wax caps are also referred to as "ballerinas" because the pink cap looks like a ballerina's tutu. The mushrooms are eatable and have a unique taste, that does resemble a fine sour cream. Primarily used by chefs for garnishing meals as they are quite rare. Herbalists tend to use them as a ground paste, to ward off any magical diseases.
Pixie’s Parasol | 5 cp | 1 lb | loot
    These mushrooms are incredibly small and quite gorgeous. Herbalists are aware they are not known to be fatal, however, they advise that they are not to be consumed, with warnings that they can cause a very upset stomach. Despite this, herbalists do harvest them in large amounts because alchemists love their looks and stock their shelves with multiple variants simply to be more pleasing to the eye, and hopefully drive a sale, as they remain visually appealing even after drying.
Plum Seeds | 1 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    The process of planting plum trees shares many similarities with that of peaches. However, it requires even more attention to temperature control. The seeds need a period of cold exposure to break dormancy, and the timing must be just right to ensure that the seeds are not exposed to too much warmth or freezing temperatures. This critical period is why only the most experienced farmhands or herbalists take on the task, ensuring that the plum seeds have the ideal conditions for successful seeding.
Popcorn Kernels | 5 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Popcorn is a variety of corn kernels that expands and puffs up when heated. The term also refers to the snack food made by heating these kernels. They can be topped with sugar, salt, butter, caramel, or cheese.
Primrose Brittlegill | 3 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    The Primrose Brittlegill appears in late summer and autumn, growing nearby to pines. This mushroom is inedible and has a 'pepper hot' taste. However, even some veteran herbalists mistake them when harvested with other types of mushrooms, like the Purple Brittlegill, which is edible.
Puffball | 5 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Puffballs are a type of mushroom featuring a ball like shape fruiting body. When fully mature, it bursts on contact or impact, releasing a cloud of spores into the surrounding area, these clouds are used by trained herbalists for further strategically inseminate the soil. They are primarily used in the making of ink, created by burning and pressing the mushroom into a fine paste.
Purple Berries | 2 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Purple berries are palpably sweet, Gatherers and Herbalists recommend eating them in small quantities, as health complications may occur. Brewmasters use them to give their ales more interesting colors, as well as make their beverages sweeter.
Purple Brittlegill | 3 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    The Purple Brittlegill is large, with a dark vinaceous or purple cap, and white or cream-colored gills, a mushroom that is edible. Herbalists recommend that due to some brittlegills being toxic, it's important to only eat mushrooms that you can recognize. If unsure, better avoid it.
Purple Emperor | 5 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Purple Emperor mushrooms have bright purple caps which shine with slime. Its name creates some confusion, as it is shared with a species of butterfly. While it is unknown if these mushrooms are edible, they are very often confused with other very similar looking mushrooms that are deadly, due to it, herbalists strongly advise against any consumption of them.
Questionable Stropharia | 1 ep | 1 lb | loot
    This mushroom that is quite often under scrutiny due to herbalists either identifying it as being edible, but also stating that it is quite poisonous, deadly even. The confusion it generates, even in controlled tests, there's a chance that it can cause violent reactions, even become deadly, but also absolutely nothing might happen. Due to that reason it also got its name.
Ramie | 5 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Ramie, also called grass linen, is a fiber derived from a type of nettle. Known for its strength and luster, it is an ideal material for creating fabrics suited to warm climates. Beyond textiles, it is also used in making fishing nets, filter cloths, and occasionally, paper.
Red Coral | 15 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Harvesting red coral is tricky and often dangerous work, yet swimmers brave the depths for it knowing there is never a shortage of buyers. Jewellers want the coral for its rich colour, shaping it into beads and ornaments, while herbalists seek it out for grinding into potions or poultices. Between the two trades, a good haul fetches a fine price at market.
Rose | 7 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Rose is a flower with many purposes for the regular folk. Symbolically, it serves as a timeless token of love, often shared on first dates or anniversaries. Practically, rose bushes are used as a gardening tool to enhance the visual appeal of parks and gardens. In some distinct regions, rose jam is coveted for its distinctive flavor.
Roseoflava | 2 gp | 0.2 lb | loot
    One of the few bioluminescent mushrooms to glow a soft pink rather than the usual greens and blues. During the day it is unremarkable, but come nightfall its caps shed a soft pink light across the forest floor. Collectors and alchemists want them, as even after collection they keep their glow.
Seed Potato | 2 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Seed potatoes are potatoes that are chosen for planting in the next growing cycle. Unlike mature potatoes that have been through several harvests, seed potatoes are harvested earlier in the growing process. While they are used for planting, they are still suitable for cooking just like regular potatoes.
Seeds - Corn | 2 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Corn seeds are small, hard kernels that come from the corn plant. They are used to grow corn, which is a staple food in many parts of the world, as well as used to create biscuits, fillings, cookies, beer, and ales. Making it a highly demanded trade good.
Seeds - Hyacinth | 3 cp | 1 lb | loot
    The small, round bulbs of the hyacinth plant were traditionally used to treat a variety of ailments, such as skin rashes, as well as in cooking in a variety of dishes. However, they are mainly sold for indoor decoration by planting them in flowerpots.
Seeds - Pumpkin | 3 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Pumpkin seeds are small, hard seeds that come from pumpkins. They can be roasted and eaten as a snack or used to make oil and other products. Pumpkin seeds might be imbued with the power to cure illnesses, making them a valuable commodity for herbalists and those seeking to improve their health.
Seeds - Sunflower | 2 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Sunflower seeds are small, hard seeds that come from the sunflower plant. They are often roasted and eaten as a snack or used to produce oil and other products.
Seeds - Wheat | 2 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Wheat seeds are small, brown seeds that come from the wheat plant. They are used to grow wheat, a grain that, when ground, is used to make bread, pasta, and other baked goods. It is also one of the most traded goods, along with corn seeds.
Silphium | 15 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Silphium, also known as laserwort, is a rare and exotic plant utilized by expert alchemists and herbalists for various purposes. Its uses include functioning as a spice, a component in perfumes, an aphrodisiac, and a medicinal remedy. It treats ailments such as coughs, sore throats, fevers, indigestion, general pain, and warts, and it also acts as a contraceptive.
Sky Blue | 3 sp | 0.1 lb | loot
    The small mushroom is a distinctive all-blue color, in some places called Werewere-kokako, due to its similar coloring to the Kokako bird. Its beauty is deceptive, for its poison brings swift death to those who dare to eat it. For the skilled artisan, it offers something different, a blue dye so rich and rare to be used for ordinary cloth to transform it into a work of art.
Slippery Jack | 3 sp | 1 lb | loot
    They are found across all the realms in pine forests and have all sorts of local names. They can be easily identified by their oily, wet appearance. Once located, herbalists recommend that Slippery Jacks must have their brown, sticky skin peeled off before eating, as this layer is not very digestible and can cause health issues.
Soot | 1 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Deep black powder or flaky substance, produced by burning material. Used by alchemists and herbalists as a binding agent.
Spicebush | 5 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    The Northern Lindera, infamous as an invasive species, is swiftly dealt with by herbalists who seek to protect local ecosystems. Yet, its fruit finds its way into kitchens, where it is appreciated as a flavorful seasoning for meats, soups, and hearty dishes, to the discontent of the herbalists.
Spiky Bloom | 2 cp | 0.2 lb | loot
    Deceptively pretty, this bloom is ringed with hardened, needle-sharp spikes that punch clean through the leather boot. Anyone who treads on one without proper protective gear rarely lives to repeat the mistake. Foragers learn to spot them quickly in their season, and avoid that part of the forest entirely.
Spring Water | 1 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Spring water is collected from underground springs and is mainly used by alchemists. It is highly sought after, and with the help of magical casters, the water is purified even further as it is generally used to create potions. Magical water streams are rare yet when found it is speculated that the potion created using this specific type of water highly increases its potency.
Star Anise | 5 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    The Star Anise is not only valued for its flavor but also for its medicinal and aromatic properties, helping with those with heart conditions. In cooking, the whole pods release their flavor gradually, making them ideal for slow-cooked dishes, while ground star anise is a main ingredient in spice mixtures such as the five-spice powder, giving dishes a sweet and spicy taste.
Stargazer Lily | 5 sp | 1 lb | loot
    "As dusk falls and the night sky begins to darken, there’s a sense of anticipation in the air. Those who know the legend watch the sky closely, waiting for the first signs of a meteor shower. At that very moment, the Stargazer Lily begins to bloom, its petals unfolding as if responding to the celestial display above. The sweet scent fills the air, carrying with it the magic of the stars as if the flower itself has been touched by the heavens." The Stargazer Lily, known for its fragrance, is grown and harvested in various regions to create perfumes. Some believe that the flower blooms only during a meteor shower, adding a mystical element to its already enchanting nature.
Sunberries | 25 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Or Wonderberries sometimes referred to as Nightshade. In the past, there had been rumors of a creature sharing the Nightshade name as well. These plants are of a universal type, Eggplants and Tomatoes are part of the same family of nightshade, as well as Tabacco and the Belladonna plant. However, when herbalists refer to Nightshade they mainly refer to a specific type called Sunberries. It is used in home remedies by the common folk.
Swine Snoot | 5 cp | 0.3 lb | loot
    Oily to the touch, this mushroom is perfectly edible, with a flavour many compare to boar meat. Before cooking, mucus substance it secretes needs be washed away. Herbalists harvest the substance, as in dire situations, when an adventurer has swallowed a toxic mushroom, it works quickly to clear out the stomach.
The Flirt | 5 sp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Russula Vesca, or The Flirt, is an edible mushroom, in fact, many people consider this to be one of the very finest wild mushrooms. The mushroom itself does come with a mild nutty flavor and could be eaten even raw, however, they do shine when they are served with meat dishes or stews.
The Last Veiled Widow | 2 gp | 0.1 lb | loot
    This highly acidic mushroom gets its name from the phosphorescent mesh that surrounds it. Compared to its more common counterpart, the Veiled Lady, the lethality of this specific mushroom is uncommon, as when ingested it turns to acid and starts consuming the unfortunate folk from the inside out, only leaving bones behind. A few months later, the bones give rise to more Veiled Widows.
The Sickener | 2 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    The Sickener, or Vomiting Russula, is a mushroom that walks the line between danger and culinary delight. Its consumption can lead to nausea and vomiting, hence its grim nickname. Yet, some regions use it as a spice in traditional dishes like goulash, relying on careful preparation to neutralize its unpleasant properties.
Toadstool | 25 gp | 1 lb | loot
    It usually has a dotted red cap and is assumed to be poisonous. If eaten it can cause serious illness and on occasion death. Harvested by gatherers and used by alchemists in creating poisons or antidotes.
Tulip | 7 cp | 1 lb | loot
    Tulips once triggered a major economic crisis, known as "tulip mania," because of their striking appearance, popularity, and limited availability. Since they required a specific climate and harvest cycle, they became luxury goods. After the bubble burst, tulips eventually became accessible at a more reasonable price, accessible to every commoner.
Verdigris Waxcap | 3 cp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Vividly green, this mushroom blends perfectly into a mossy forest floor, being easy to overlook and easier still to gather by mistake. The camouflage is what makes it dangerous, as the mushroom is highly toxic. Herbalists advise foragers to learn of its cap well and to leave any green mushroom they cannot name where it stands.
Violet Webcap | 3 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Commonly known as the Violet Webcap or Violet Cort, this mushroom is easily distinguished from many other types due to its unique purple color and a cap that feels like short hairs to the touch. This makes it quite distinct from other mushrooms that share a similar coloring. While it is not toxic, extreme caution is advised because it can easily be mistaken for more toxic brethren. Even so, commoners report that it tastes like chewed tobacco, so it is not an actively harvested mushroom.
Water Lily | 1 sp | 0.2 lb | loot
    Though many manors have the water lily as decoration, floating across their large fountains and ornamental ponds, the flower has different uses as well. Herbalists gather and grind the blooms, adding them to the mix of ingredients that goes into their poultices.
Wild Mint | 1 sp | 1 lb | loot
    The leaves are a popular choice to garnish some beverages and desserts. Gatherers farm huge quantities of Wild Mint as they can also be made into teas to treat colds and to aid digestion.
Witchhat Mushroom | 2 sp | 1 lb | loot
    Due to the rarity of this specific mushroom, most herbalists are uncertain of its toxicity. This uncertainty persists because those who may have sampled it did not survive to tell the tale, or, when attempts are made to test it, none can be found for study. Some attribute its rarity to arcane influences, a belief based on superstition stemming from its name.
Wolf Bane's Leaves | 250 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Referred to as Belladonna, it is usually believed to be of aid when communing with deities, as it is known that it can bring forth a strong connection to any plane. Alchemists usually harvest it with utmost caution as it is used in the creation of one of the deadliest plant-derived poisons on the market. Herbalists use it as a cure for early forms of Lycanthropy and as a treatment to manage and delay the more advanced stages of the disease.
Wood Lily | 5 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Gathered from mountainous regions, they are said to be quite toxic for small home animals, however, herbalists use their bulbs crushed on swellings and bruises, or provide them as medicinal teas for patients who complain of stomachaches or coughs and fevers.
Yellow Stainer | 3 sp | 1 lb | loot
    It is easily identified by a bright yellow stain that appears immediately when the cap is bruised or cut, along with a distinct smell resembling to ink. The herbalists recommend as this should not be eaten, while being not deadly, it does upset the stomach, and there's a possibility of permanent damage to it.

== Tools & Craft / Instruments (26) ==

Arpa Doppia | 10 gp | 5 lb | loot
    The Arpa Doppia, or double harp, renowned for its intricate double strings, delivers a one-of-a-kind sound, cherished by the upper echelons of society and frequently gracing the vibrant, bustling cities across the realms.
Bamboula | 5 gp | 10 lb | loot
    The bamboula, a drum made from a rum barrel covered with skin on one end, is also associated with a traditional dance that shares its name. In communities that embrace the bamboula, its music and dance foster an exceptionally close-knit spirit. While the instrument is common in island nations, it's not unusual for traveling bards or historians to encounter it outside those regions.
Berimbau | 5 gp | 2 lb | loot
    Resembling a wooden hunting bow, the instrument comprises the verga referred to as the bow, a steel string, and a dried, hollowed calabash attached to its lower end. It is most commonly played by martial artists who practice capoeira. This distinctive fighting style integrates music and dance as part of its movements.
Concertina | 2 gp | 2 lb | loot
    The concertina, the smaller sister to the accordion, is a favorite among seafaring folk due to its compact size, pleasing sound, and easy repairs. Made primarily from giant reed, which grows along the shores of deep rivers, this material is readily available during the ship’s travels, ensuring quick fixes when entertainment is needed.
Cornet | 5 gp | 2 lb | loot
    The cornet sometimes called the circular trumpet or natural cornet, is primarily crafted from brass by master blacksmiths in specialized forges. Its unique valve system ensures a clear and resonant sound, making precision in its manufacturing essential for skilled smiths.
Djembe | 4 gp | 2 lb | loot
    The djembe, sometimes referred to as simply the jembe, is a rope-tuned drum played with bare hands. Its name, in some areas, translates to 'everyone gathers together in peace', embodying unity and community spirit. The drum’s shell is carved from hardwood, while its head is traditionally made from untreated goat rawhide, reflecting its deep cultural roots.
Fiddle | 3 gp | 2 lb | loot
    The fiddle, more commonly known as the violin, is often used for music that focuses on dancing. The distinction between the two lies in how the music is played. The fiddle is characterized by fast rhythms and quick note changes, while the violin typically produces a more mellow sound with longer, sustained notes. The instrument’s name depends on the style in which it is played.
Flute | 1 gp | 2 lb | loot
    The flute is known for its adaptability, being made from a wide range of materials such as bamboo, jade, glass, blackwood, bone, and various metals. Its light, bright sound has made it a beloved instrument in many cultures, especially in tavern settings where it often forms part of a trio with the lute and harp. Beyond its popularity in taverns, the flute is also widely used in rituals, celebrations, and folk traditions, giving it a strong cultural presence in both formal and informal events across the realms.
Gandingan | 15 gp | 20 lb | loot
    The gandingan is a set of four large, hanging gongs used for percussion in theater plays, often to heighten suspense or in traditional musical performances. Made primarily of bronze, the gongs vary in size and are crafted with thin bodies and wide rims, allowing for convenient stacking during transportation as they tend to be quite heavy.
Gong | 75 gp | 0.01 lb | loot
    The gong is a flat, circular metal disc typically struck with a mallet. It is prudced out of bronze, brass, or iron and is suspended on a stand. Gongs do vary in size, from small to large, and can either be pre-tuned or require manual tuning. As an instrument, the gong has multiple uses, ranging from opera performances to serving as a signal device in various settings, such as for events like fights or races, or even as alarms.
Hand Bell | 2 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Cast from bronze or brass, tuned by shaving metal off the inside of the rim until it os tuned to a specific note. Temples, schools, and town criers all need them, for announcements, sermons or classes starting. A cracked bell cannot be repaired and goes straight back into the crucible to be made into a new one.
Horn | 5 gp | 2 lb | loot
    The horn is often regarded as the predecessor to both the trumpet and the cornet. While it is primarily made from brass, there are also variants crafted from bone, which are particularly favored in remote, cold, or mountainous regions. They can be used for music, drive for battles, or alarms.
Hosho | 1 gp | 2 lb | loot
    The hosho is a rattle instrument crafted from a wild orange known as damba, with sticks used to tie it together and filled with hota seeds or pebbles. Its unique shape produces a sharp, distinctive sound that often cuts through the music of other instruments.
Huni Guitar | 15 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Unlike a regular guitar, this instrument has a square shape that affects its acoustics. The sound hole creates a slight reverb effect on the strings, giving the guitar a distinctive tone compared to other stringed instruments. Bards often include these guitars in musical ensembles because of their unique sound.
Hurdy-Gurdy | 5 gp | 2 lb | loot
    The hurdy-gurdy is a string instrument that creates sound by using a hand-crank-turned, round rosined wheel that rubs against the strings. Melodies are produced on a keyboard, that presses tangents against one or more strings to alter their pitch. This unique mechanism allows for a continuous melody while notes are played, giving the instrument its characteristic sound.
Kinnor | 2 gp | 2 lb | loot
    The kinnor resembles the lyre in shape, but it is typically held from the end and strummed like a lute. Unlike the lyre, where fingerpicking is common, the kinnor uses a metal rod as a pick, allowing for the production of prolonged notes and a distinct sound.
Lyre | 2 gp | 2 lb | loot
    The lyre is one of the oldest string instruments, characterized by its yoke, which consists of two arms and a crossbar that projects horizontally from the body. While most lyres are plucked to produce sound, rarely, a number of them are designed to be bowed, adding to their versatility in music.
Maracas | 1 gp | 2 lb | loot
    Known by various names like chac-chac, tamaracas, or shakers, maracas are rattle instruments usually played in pairs. They contain a few pebbles inside and are traditionally covered with the skin of a rare bird called the scarlet ibis. Many believe that the dances accompanied by the maracas can heal the sick.
Maram | 3 gp | 5 lb | loot
    The maram is a percussion instrument found in various regions. It features a wooden cylinder with two skin heads on either end, which are tensioned with skin laces to create sound. This construction allows the maram to produce a rich, resonant tone when played to be used in a variety of musical situations such as traditional ceremonies to dance performances.
Mbira | 15 sp | 5 lb | loot
    The mbira is usually made from several types of hardwoods that form its soundboard, with iron tines for sound production. Revered in many cultures, the mbira is thought to produce sounds that can calm the mind and dispel magical thoughts, although this idea is largely based on folklore and remains only a rumor.
Pandean Pipe | 12 sp | 5 lb | loot
    The Pandean pipe, often referred to as the pan flute or panpipe, is made from closed tubes of increasing lengths. Sound is produced when air is blown in its tubes, causing them to resonate and produce a unique sound. This instrument is widely used in music and traditional performances.
Piano | 100 gp | 5 lb | loot
    The piano is a keyboard instrument that generates sound by striking strings with wooden hammers when its keys are pressed. Its production is quite expensive due to the need for skilled craftsmanship, involving experienced carpenters, skilled blacksmiths, and adept machinists. The piano plays a significant role in various musical genres, serving as both a solo instrument and an essential component in orchestras and ensembles for high-class citizens.
Tablàh | 12 sp | 5 lb | loot
    The tablàh, commonly referred to as the goblet drum or chalice drum, and sometimes called dombek or tarabuka depending on the region, has a shape that resembles a large goblet. Made of wood with a thin leather membrane, this percussion instrument plays a vital role in many musical traditions, contributing a unique rhythm to them.
Tambourine | 5 sp | 5 lb | loot
    The tambourine is a versatile percussion instrument featuring a wooden frame with pairs of small metal jingles known as zills. It can be played in many ways such as held in the hand, mounted on a stand, shaken to produce a lively sound, or struck sharply with the hand or a stick. Musicians can also use the tambourine attached to their leg or hip for added rhythm.
Timpani | 2 gp | 5 lb | loot
    Timpani, frequently referred to as kettledrums, are percussion instruments characterized by their striking sound. Played with specialized drumsticks known as mallets, held in pairs. Musicians often have a collection of timpani mallets on hand, as each mallet produces a unique timbre and sound. The variety enhances their role in orchestral music, where they provide a one-of-a-kind rhythmic experience.
Zither | 5 gp | 5 lb | loot
    The zither is an instrument that exists in various shapes and sizes, with the defining characteristic of being a plucked instrument. Numerous regional variations can be found worldwide. In some cultures, the zither is celebrated as an all-rounded instrument, valued for its beautiful and resonant sound. This instrument is not only a staple in music but is also well-regarded in some religious settings, where its beautiful sound enhances choir performances.
