== Apparel (21) ==

Bycocket Hat | 1 gp | 1 lb | equipment
    Stiff leather shaped wet over a form and left to cure, giving a wide brim that is turned up in the back and pointed in the front like a bird's beak. The hat is worn by nobles and royalty, but also can be see worn by the merchant class.
Common Robes | 1 gp | 1 lb | equipment
    Simple linen garments made usually by unskilled tailors, which is precisely why they remain so widespread among commoners, scholars, or the clergy. Most are left undyed, rarely some are treated with cheap dyes to achieve muted browns or grays for those who want slight variation.
Common Sash | 1 gp | 1 lb | equipment
    Woven from linen or cotton, or cut from leather, and tied around the waist or over the shoulder, it can be used for holding a piece of clothing in place or to mark an affiliation with a trade guild or local militia. Street shopkeepers sell them in bundles for next to nothing, which is why nearly everyone seems to own at least one.
Common Scarf | 1 gp | 1 lb | equipment
    Rectangular cloth made from usually from wool, produced in such great quantities that it holds almost no resale value. Shepherds, farmers, and market workers are rarely seen without one, as it serves equally well to wipe sweat, filter dusty air, or wrap around the face during cold winds.
Common Shoes | 1 gp | 1 lb | equipment
    Basic footwear made from a single piece of hide or cheap leather, stitched together with sinew or hemp. Shoemakers produce them quickly and in large batches, as they are easy to do in bulk. Experienced cobblers can tell how far a traveler has walked by the state of the soles alone.
Corset | 1 gp | 1 lb | equipment
    Structured piece of clothing made from layered linen reinforced with thin strips of wood or iron, or in wealthier households, steel, laced tightly to shape the torso. While most commonly associated with noble attire, many working women also wear cheaper versions for back support during long hours of work.
Crakows | 1 gp | 1 lb | equipment
    Long, pointed shoes made from soft leather, with tips extending well beyond the toes, sometimes requiring stuffing or wire attached to the knee to prevent dragging. Fully impractical for physical work or travel, which is precisely the point, as wearing them tells that one has no need for either.
Decorative Corset | 1 gp | 1 lb | equipment
    Made from materials such as silk or dyed linen, with added embroidery, lacework, or metal accents, while skilled tailors spending weeks, if not more, on a single piece. Nobles and wealthy merchants commission them for formal occasions, where comfort is secondary to appearance, and most wearers tolerate the restriction at the price of presentation.
Evening Gloves | 1 gp | 1 lb | equipment
    Long gloves made from thin leather or silk, dyed in visible colors, such as reds or greens, extending past the wrist and reaching the elbow. Owning a pair in good condition is a statement of wealth, as bare hands are frowned upon at formal gatherings by certain social circles.
Fez | 1 gp | 1 lb | equipment
    Flat topped hat made from stiffened felt, usually in a red, finished with a silk tassel at the base. It has become something of a recognizable symbol of trade culture in the realms, and in many port cities, spotting one in a crowd usually means a merchant is nearby looking to do business, under the hand or otherwise.
Ghoonghat | 1 gp | 1 lb | equipment
    Veil made from a long section of fabric that goes over the head and across the face, with material varying from simple cotton among commoners to richly embroidered silk among the wealthy. It is traditionally worn as a sign of modesty or respect in certain cultures across the realms, particularly in the presence of elders or during ceremonies.
Hoop Skirt | 1 gp | 1 lb | equipment
    An wide, bell shaped skirt held in its exaggerated form by a framework of rings made from thin steel wire sewn into the fabric. Exclusively a piece of clothing for the wealthy, as the amount of material required to cover the frame alone makes it expensive to produce.
Lapel Trim Hat | 1 gp | 1 lb | equipment
    An brimmed hat with decorative trimming along the lapel, usually ribbon, feathers, or embroidered cloth secured with a small pin or brooch. Merchants and minor nobility favor them for public appearances, as the design of the trim alone can communicate one's nobility status without a word being said.
Leather Boots | 1 gp | 1 lb | equipment
    Sturdy footwear made from thick cowhide, treated with animal fat or beeswax to resist water. Soldiers, couriers, and seasoned travelers consider a good pair an investment worth more than weapons, as blistered feet on the road can be as dangerous as any bandit.
Miner Padded Clothes | 1 gp | 1 lb | equipment
    Heavy clothing stuffed with raw wool or cotton batting, stitched between two layers of tough material to cushion against falling debris and scraping tunnel walls. Most miners own only one set and repair it endlessly, as replacing them costs more coin than may earn in a week.
Miner's Hat | 1 gp | 1 lb | equipment
    Hardened cap reinforced with strips of iron or thick stitching, fitted with a small bracket at the front for mounting a candle or oil wick, or support for specialized mining glasses. It is one of the few pieces of equipment a miner is never without, as working in the dark without one is considered not brave but foolish by anyone who has spent any time underground.
Stylized Girdle | 1 gp | 1 lb | equipment
    An ornamental belt crafted from tooled leather or woven fabric, set with polished gemstones or metal studs, worn low on the hips over robes or dresses. In many noble houses, the craftsmanship of one's girdle is scrutinized as closely as the clothing beneath it.
Tiered Dress | 1 gp | 1 lb | equipment
    Multiple layered dress constructed from progressively larges rolls of material stitched at intervals down the skirt, creating a cascading effect. Seamstresses and tailors charge considerably more for them, opposed to regular dressers, due to the amount of fabric and precise stitching required.
Top Hat | 1 gp | 1 lb | equipment
    Tall hat made from stiffened felt or small animal pelt, shaped over a wooden log. It is almost always associated with wealth and urban society, and wearing one in a rural village is a reliable way to draw either respect or suspicion, depending on where you are.
Waist Cincher | 1 gp | 1 lb | equipment
    Shorter and less restrictive than a corset, made from stiffened fabric or light leather, designed to compress and shape only the waist rather than the full torso. Many performers and dancers prefer them than corsets, as the restriction of movement is much more limited.
Winter Hat | 1 gp | 1 lb | equipment
    Crafted from thick wool or fur, lined with small animal pelt, and pulled low to cover the ears and the back of the neck. Northern traders sell them in large batches at the onset of the cold months, and those who wait too long to buy one usually end up paying quite a bit for the last of the stock.

Far North Crown | 200 gp | 1 lb | loot
    Far North Crown is not a symbol of authority but rather one of dominance over the frozen wasteland. Generally made from iron, and on some occasion even from pelt, the crown does not signify that the wearer rules people, but that they have mastered the land itself.
Far North Dye | 25 gp | 0.1 lb | loot
    Far North Dye is ground from purple ochre, a rare variant of the ochre gemstone, which is ground into a fine powder. Though uncommon in the region, it is one of the most frequently used dyes in the northern realms, as it provides strong visibility from a distance on the light colored pelts it is typically applied to.
Far North Epaulets | 0 gp | 0 lb | equipment
